A based transported slot under an arbitrary local gauge transformation changes only by the adjoint action of that gauge transformation at its base point: the interior transports cancel in pairs. Averaging over the anchor group therefore makes the read invariant under the full local gauge action. Finally the polynomial \(q\) attached to the averaged read is itself gauge invariant, hence a label: for a constant gauge transformation \(g\), the rotated polynomial has the same Taylor forms as \(f \circ g = f\), and the polynomial with given forms through order \(d\) is unique. This produces the required \(f\) and \(q\).\(\Box\)

The proposition applies to the actual action, rather than to a formal substitute: the slot germ is obtained from the link ODE above. Its one-factor linearization is the oriented plaquette sum. The finite-generation formula for the ideal is obtained degree by degree by expanding each polynomial into monomials, applying the projection onto the Maxwell solution-potential subspace, and splitting one literal equation factor from the remaining slot coordinates. The safe-span annihilator proof handles the edge avoidance needed to multiply the spectators. These steps establish the positioned read.

Theorem 13.9. (Marked lifting and equivariant marked lifting at fixed cutoff) Assume at cutoff \(d\) there is a complex-linear map \(S\) from labels to link-field functions with unit value one, gauge-invariant values, and exact action Taylor forms through order \(d\). In addition, for each pure-grade basis label of grade \((r, s)\), assume its value has a finite representation as a linear combination of products of based curvature-slot color coordinates, each product having at least \(s\) factors. This is the powered slot-degree condition used below. Then there is a marked lifting \(F\) at cutoff \(d\) in the sense defined above: \(F\) is complex linear, \(F (1) = (1, 1)\), and for every pure-grade label \(p\) of grade \(m = (r, s)\) with \(r, s \leqslant d\) the value \(F (p) = (q_p, f_p)\) consists of a gauge-invariant function \(f_p\) of slot degree at least \(s\) and a label \(q_p\) with the same action Taylor forms through order \(d\), such that \(q_p - p\) has strictly larger stamp than \(m\), and such that \(f_p\) is microscopic whenever \(p\) lies in the equation ideal. The marked lifting may be chosen to be an equivariant marked lifting, that is, to commute with signed coordinate changes and the real structure.

Proof. We first construct the unsymmetrized marked lifting. The sound section itself provides a linear map \(X\) with

\(\displaystyle Xp = (p, S (p)), \qquad X 1 = (1, 1) .\)

It gives the required action forms, gauge invariance, and slot degree for each pure grade. It need not be microscopic on the equation ideal.

For a fixed grade \(m\), restrict the input \(p\) to \(\text{Grade} (m)\) and let

\(\displaystyle P_m = \text{Grade} (m) \cap \mathcal{I}_d,\)

where \(\mathcal{I}_d\) is the finite-dimensional Maxwell equation ideal. Choose a basis \((b_i)\) of \(P_m\). For each \(b_i\), apply Proposition 13.8 and write its read as \((q_i, f_i)\). Extend \(b_i \mapsto (q_i, f_i)\) linearly on \(P_m\). The result is a payment map on the ideal part of this grade: forms, strict-stamp correction, powered membership, and the Micro conclusion are all linear conditions and hence pass through the basis expansion.

Choose a linear projection \(L_m\) from the full label space onto \(P_m\) which restricts to the identity on \(P_m\). If \(X_m\) is the map just constructed on \(P_m\) and \(X\) is the sound-section map above, set

\(\displaystyle F_m (p) = X_m (L_m p) + X (p - L_m p) .\)

Both summands have the required grade-\(m\) payment, so their sum does too. If this grade-\(m\) input \(p\) lies in the equation ideal, grade preservation of the ideal gives \(L_m p \in P_m\) and \(p - L_m p \in P_m\). Since \(L_m\) is the identity on \(P_m\), applying \(L_m\) to \(p - L_m p\) gives zero and also \(p - L_m p\); therefore \(p - L_m p = 0\). Thus for ideal inputs the entire value is the microscopic marked value \(X_m (p)\). If \(m = (0, 0)\), the unit is not in the equation ideal: the ideal has no nonzero element of grade \((0, 0)\), because such an element is a constant and every element of the ideal vanishes at the zero jet. Hence \(P_{(0, 0)} = 0\), so \(L_m 1 = 0\) and the sound map supplies \(F_m (1) = (1, 1)\).

There are only finitely many relevant grades,

\(\displaystyle \{0, \ldots, d\} \times \{0, \ldots, d\} .\)

Decompose \(p = \sum_m p_m\) by its homogeneous grade and define \(F (p) = \sum_m F_m (p_m)\). On an input of pure grade \(m\), every other grade component vanishes, so the corresponding payment remains exact. On the unit only grade \((0, 0)\) contributes, so the start condition holds. This is the claimed marked lifting.

Now symmetrize. Let \(\mathcal{S}\) be the finite signed coordinate permutation group. Its action \(\theta_s\) on labels is the substitution \((\theta_s q) (J) = q (s^{- 1} J)\) of the rotated jet, and its joint action on a label/function pair is \(\phi_s (q, f) = (\theta_s q, \hspace{0.17em} f \circ \text{pull}_s)\), where \(\text{pull}_s\) is the pullback of cover configurations under the signed lattice map \(s\) fixing the anchor. Define

\(\displaystyle F^{\text{sgn}} = \frac{1}{|\mathcal{S}|} \sum_{s \in \mathcal{S}} \phi_s F \theta_{s^{- 1}} .\)

Signed changes preserve grade, the strictly higher-stamp subspace, the slot-product filtration, the action-form identity, and the equation ideal. They preserve the moment condition by changing variables in the finite volume measure. Thus every summand remains a marked lifting and the average is one. Reindexing by \(s \mapsto ts\) shows \(F^{\text{sgn}} \theta_t = \phi_t F^{\text{sgn}}\).

Let \(c\) be the joint real-conjugation involution on labels and label/function pairs: on labels it conjugates the coefficients of the polynomial in the real jet coordinates, and \(c (q, f) = (\bar{q}, \bar{f})\). It is conjugate-linear, commutes with the signed action, and preserves each marked lifting condition. Set

\(\displaystyle F^{\text{imp}} = \tfrac{1}{2} (F^{\text{sgn}} + cF^{\text{sgn}} c) .\)

The midpoint is complex-linear because the two conjugate-linear actions on the source and target cancel. It remains a marked lifting; \(c^2 = 1\) gives real equivariance, while commutation of \(c\) with the signed action retains signed equivariance. Both averages fix the unit. This is the required equivariant marked lifting.\(\Box\)

The exact algebraic assembly above uses no limit in volume and no compactness argument. Its analytic input is the positioned read. Proposition 13.8 supplies that input using the radial ODE germ, the finite Taylor annihilator argument, the equation-ideal generation formula, and the conditional-Haar microscopic identity above. The following corollary uses the section construction.

Remark 13.10. (Mixed grades) The marked lifting conditions are stated for labels of pure grade. For an arbitrary element \(z\) of the equation ideal, \(F (z) = \sum_m F (z_m)\) over its homogeneous components, and every \(z_m\) again lies in the ideal, because the substitution \(\text{changed} (\mathcal{E})\) defining the ideal preserves both grades. Hence the function component of \(F (z)\) is a finite sum of microscopic functions. It is microscopic, with the largest of the finitely many radii and side thresholds.

Corollary 13.11. (Actual fixed-cutoff equivariant marked lifting) For every gauge group and every cutoff \(d\), and for test holonomies fixed as at the beginning of this section, the actual section construction yields a marked lifting and an equivariant marked lifting at that cutoff.

Proof. Use the local-sections construction of Subsection 7.10. For cutoff \(d\) it gives the section \(T_d F\) and pose polynomials \(P_{\alpha}\) with exact action forms through order \(d\), unit, and gauge covariance. Its pose certificate says that every monomial in the pose polynomial of a grade-\((r, s)\) basis label has total factor degree at least \(s\). Expanding the finite pose polynomial writes each such basis value as a finite sum of slot-coordinate products with at least \(s\) factors. Thus the powered slot-degree hypothesis of the theorem holds. Applying the theorem gives a marked lifting; its finite signed and real averages give the equivariant marked lifting.\(\Box\)

13.6A fixed marked section and its calibration budgets

The marked finite-volume route needs a single local section at each label degree. That section must be chosen before the volume, cutoff, or derivative budget is known. It must also bound every later derivative request at that degree. We construct that section first, then show how the schedule selects its budgets and how the approximate field change is made invertible.

13.6.1One section for every later derivative budget

Let \(H\) be the fixed holomorphic gauge action. At a fixed degree \(d\), let \(E_d\) be a finite-dimensional complex label space with a real homogeneous basis \((e_j)_{j \in J_d}\). The basis contains the unit and has grades \((r_j, s_j)\) with \(r_j \leqslant d\). The algebra used here is not filtered by raw link-entry count. Let \(\mathfrak{S}\) be the collection of based and transported curvature slots on the cover lattice, let \(I\) be the real color-coordinate basis, and put \(\text{Chip} =\mathfrak{S} \times I\). For \(c = (S, a) \in \text{Chip}\) let \(x_c (U)\) be color coordinate \(a\) of curvature slot \(S\) at configuration \(U\). Define \(\mathcal{P}_{\geqslant s}\) as the complex span of the finite products \(\prod_{\nu = 1}^m x_{c_{\nu}}\) with \(m \geqslant s\). Thus one factor means one curvature-coordinate slot, even though it is itself polynomial in link entries. This is the source filtration called \(\text{Powered}\).

The input to the section construction is an equivariant marked lifting: linear maps \(A_d : E_d \to E_d\) and \(B_d : E_d \to \mathcal{P}_{\geqslant 0}\) with unit normalization \(A_d 1 = 1\), \(B_d 1 = 1\), gauge invariance of \(B_d p\), \(\text{Forms} (H, d, B_d p, A_d p)\), and \(B_d p \in \text{Micro}\) whenever \(p\) belongs to the equation ideal, together with the marked lifting's exact-action identity. They also have the following filtration clauses. The stamp of a grade is

\(\displaystyle \text{st} (r, s) = (s, r),\)

and stamps are compared lexicographically in the displayed order. Say that a label has stamp at least \(\text{st} (r, s)\) (respectively strictly above it) if each of its nonzero homogeneous components, of grade \((r', s')\) say, has \(\text{st} (r', s') \geqslant \text{st} (r, s)\) (respectively \(>\)). If \(p\) has stamp at least \(\text{st} (r, s)\), then \(A_d p - p\) has stamp strictly above \(\text{st} (r, s)\), and \(B_d p \in \mathcal{P}_{\geqslant s}\). By linearity it is equivalent to require these two clauses for homogeneous \(p\) of grade \((r, s)\) only: a component of grade \((r', s')\) with larger stamp has \(s' \geqslant s\), because increasing the stamp never lowers the factor coordinate, and \(\mathcal{P}_{\geqslant s'} \subseteq \mathcal{P}_{\geqslant s}\). In addition, \(A_d\) and \(B_d\) each commute with the signed-coordinate action and the real structure; equivariance of only \(B_d\) would not suffice to pass it through \(A_d^{- 1}\). A marked lifting without these two equivariances is turned into such an equivariant marked lifting by the finite group and conjugation averages recalled next; the marked lifting producer itself is a separate input to this local theorem. It is constructed in Subsection 13.5.

For clarity, the symmetrization just mentioned is finite-dimensional. If \(\Phi_s\) is the joint signed action on labels and fields, and \(\mathfrak{c}\) is the joint real-conjugation involution, put

\(\displaystyle F_0 = \frac{1}{|\mathcal{S}|} \sum_{s \in \mathcal{S}} \Phi_s \hspace{0.17em} F \hspace{0.17em} \theta_{s^{- 1}}, \qquad F_1 = \frac{1}{2} (F_0 +\mathfrak{c}F_0 \mathfrak{c}),\)

where \(\mathcal{S}\) is the signed-coordinate group. Each summand is still a marked lifting: the grade is preserved, the correction remains strictly above its stamp, the field remains in \(\mathcal{P}_{\geqslant s}\), and the unit and exact action identities are preserved. A finite average preserves these linear conditions. Reindexing the first sum by left multiplication shows that \(F_0\) commutes with every signed action; the involution identity \(\mathfrak{c}^2 = 1\) shows \(F_1\) commutes with conjugation, and the two actions commute, so both equivariances hold for \(F_1\). Both averages fix the unit. This is the mathematical content of passing from a marked lifting to an equivariant marked lifting; the existence of the unsymmetrized marked lifting from the action is not reproved in this section.

Theorem 13.12. (Fixed-stencil family of fixed marked sections from an equivariant marked lifting) Given an equivariant marked lifting on \(E_d\), there are a finite centered stencil \(S_d\), a linear section

\(\displaystyle T_d : E_d \longrightarrow \mathbb{C} [\text{link entries on } S_d],\)

and an invertible linear regrading \(R_d : E_d \to E_d\) such that

  1. \(0 \in S_d\) and \(S_d\) is invariant under signed coordinate permutations;

  2. evaluating \(T_d\) at the coordinates of \(R_d p\) on every link field gives \(B_d p\) exactly;

  3. \(T_d\) is gauge invariant, has the correct unit, is compatible with signed pullbacks and conjugation, and has the prescribed action projection;

  4. each basis entry has a finite pose supported in \(S_d\) whose degree is at least \(s_j\);

  5. for every derivative order \(k\) and power \(P\), there is a finite number \(C_{d, k, P}\) such that each basis entry has section rate \(C_{d, k, P}\) at factor degree \(s_j\). This is a bound on the healthy part of the translated stencil, for every budget point of order \(k\) and power \(P\), torus, anchor, and tested direction list allowed by that budget point;

  6. one may take \(R_d = A_d\). Then \(R_d\) commutes with the signed-coordinate action and with the real structure, and for every \(p\) in the equation ideal the field obtained by evaluating \(T_d\) at the coordinates of \(R_d p\) belongs to \(\text{Micro}\).

The section and stencil depend on \(d\) and the fixed action/frame only; they do not depend on \(k, P\), torus size, or cutoff.

Proof. First invert the triangular regrading. On the finite grade decomposition, the condition that \(A_d p - p\) has strictly higher grade makes \(A_d\) an upper-triangular block matrix with identity diagonal. Back substitution solves \(A_d x = y\) grade by grade: at the lowest grade, \(x\) has the same component as \(y\); after subtracting that component, the next grade is solved in the same way. Finiteness of the grade set makes this process terminate and gives a unique \(x\). Thus \(R_d = A_d\) is invertible, and the same induction shows that its inverse preserves the lower grade bound.

For each basis vector set \(f_j = B_d (R_d^{- 1} e_j)\). By the filtration property, \(f_j\) is a finite linear combination of monomials, each with at least \(s_j\) curvature-coordinate factors. Each monomial uses only a finite list of slots. A product is represented by concatenating its two lists of slots. A sum is represented by taking the disjoint union of the lists and adding the corresponding polynomials. The empty list represents constants. Induction over the finite monomial expansion therefore gives, for each \(j\), a finite slot list and a pose polynomial of degree at least \(s_j\) realizing \(f_j\).

There are only finitely many basis vectors. The union of their slot stencils is finite, so choose a centered box containing it. Enlarge the box symmetrically if needed; this preserves containment and makes the stencil invariant under signed coordinate permutations. On this one box, the polynomial realization is constructed for every basis vector. Extend these values linearly from the basis to define \(T_d\). Linearity gives the exact evaluation identity for every \(p\), not merely for basis labels.

The evaluation identities also transfer all structure. Gauge invariance, the unit normalization, signed covariance, conjugation reality, and the action-projection condition are the corresponding properties of \(B_d\). Because evaluation agrees on the entire basis, the section has these properties on all labels. The basis poses give the common-support and degree witnesses. The last item of the theorem is immediate from the choice \(R_d = A_d\): the two equivariances of \(A_d\) are equivariant marked lifting hypotheses, and by the exact evaluation identity the field attached to \(R_d p\) is \(B_d p\), which is microscopic for \(p\) in the equation ideal.

It remains to show the derivative budgets for all later queries. The section rate statement is localized: for a stencil \(S\), order \(k\), power \(P\), side \(n\), and anchor \(o\), it controls derivatives after expansion at \(o\) on \(\text{Healthy} (\theta, S + o)\), for direction lists obeying the budget point's direction bound at \(o\). The extra derivative-rate parameter here is zero, so the threshold factor remains exactly \(\theta^s\) at every tested derivative order. Each color-coordinate curvature slot has rate degree one: on configurations healthy on the translated stencil, the slot and each of its tested derivatives through order \(k\) have norm at most a constant times \(\theta\), uniformly in the torus and the anchor. This is the slot estimate proved in Subsection 7.10 (the value is bounded by health and the isometry of parallel transport, and every derivative of positive order \(\ell\) by a constant times \(\theta^{\ell} \leqslant \theta\) because each tested direction on a link of the stencil has size at most a constant times \(\theta\)). A real color coordinate is a bounded linear functional of the slot, so it obeys the same bound. For a binary product, the product rule gives at most \(2^k\) terms at order \(k\). Iterating over a product of \(m\) factors gives at most \(m^k\) terms; in each pose the number \(m\) is fixed, so this changes only its finite constant. Factor degrees add under multiplication. Hence a monomial of at least \(s_j\) chips has rate degree at least \(s_j\). Finite sums add their constants, and lowering a rate from a larger degree to \(s_j\) is valid because \(0 < \theta \leqslant 1\). Applying this to the finite pose expansion of each basis field gives one finite constant \(c_j (k, P)\) for the exact localized rate above. Summing basis estimates with coefficient moduli gives the linear bound for arbitrary labels. No uniformity in \(d, k\), or \(P\) is claimed.\(\Box\)

Item (5) is the currency furnished by the finite section. In the application, the profile at stage \(i\) requests the particular order \(k_i\) and power \(P_i\) appearing in that profile. For each \(d \leqslant i\), choose one available amount \(a_{di} \geqslant 0\) for this request. It remains a valid credit after replacing a negative amount by zero, since all budgets are nonnegative. The section is not rebuilt when these requests change.

13.6.2Buying all stage and degree conditions

For a stage \(i\), let \(c_i\) be its column count, let \(q_d\) be the stencil size of the chosen section together with the fixed local section, and let \(\alpha_i\) be its support exponent. The sphere bound is

\(\displaystyle \textsf{S}_i (q_d, a) = 2 + e^{81 \alpha_i} (a + c_i + 2 q_d) .\)

For each fixed pair \(d \leqslant i\), the chosen credit \(a_{di}\), the sphere bound, and the coefficient \(2 (1 + c_i)\) are finite. The orbit cost of the degree-\(d\) frame,

\(\displaystyle \mathcal{O}_d = 1 + \sum_g \sum_{j \in J_d} \| \rho_g e_j \|_1,\)

where \(g\) runs over the finite list of signed coordinate permutations with or without conjugation and \(\rho_g\) is its action on coefficient vectors, is finite and at least one. Hence its sliver

\(\displaystyle \delta_d = \frac{1}{2 \hspace{0.17em} \mathcal{O}_d^2}\)

is strictly positive. Since the volume purse is \(\textsf{P} (L) = L^{1 / 8} \to \infty\), choose an integer \(n_{di}\) such that

\(\displaystyle 2 (1 + c_i) \textsf{S}_i (q_d, a_{di}) \leqslant \textsf{P} (L) \delta_d \qquad (L \geqslant n_{di}) .\)

The credit clause and nonnegativity hold at every such \(L\). Separately, for each \(j\) choose \(v_j\) so that

\(\displaystyle (1 + 2 c_j) \hspace{0.17em} \textsf{D} (L) \leqslant \overline{\textsf{P}} (L) \qquad (L \geqslant v_j),\)

where the interface toll and purse are \(\textsf{D} (L) = \text{rate} (L) = L^{- 2 / 5}\) and \(\overline{\textsf{P}} (L) = \text{stepSmall} (L) = L^{- 3 / 8}\). Their ratio is \(L^{- 1 / 40}\), so the inequality holds once \(L^{1 / 40} \geqslant 1 + 2 c_j\). For a fixed stage \(i\), take the maximum of the finitely many \(n_{di}\) with \(d \leqslant i\) and \(v_j\) with \(j \leqslant i\). This single threshold \(n_i\) proves all clauses required at stage \(i\) simultaneously. The resulting nonnegative table \(a_{di}\) and thresholds \(n_i\) are exactly the same section's all-stage purchase data.

The later delayed budget has a similar finite-threshold argument. Write \(\beta_F (x, L) = x + b_L\), where \(b_L\) is the fixed logarithmic scale shift, and write \(\textsf{f}_d (x) = x^{- w_d}\) for the reciprocal tail. For the public schedule \(w_d = \max_j s_j + 4\), the maximal factor grade of the degree-\(d\) frame plus four; the following delay argument works for any fixed positive integer exponent. The tail comparison needed by the delay is, with \(y = \beta_F (x, L) = x + b_L\),

\(\displaystyle y^{- 1} L^{d + 1} \hspace{0.17em} \textsf{f}_d (x) \leqslant \textsf{f}_d (y) .\)

For fixed \(L, d\), and all sufficiently large \(x\), one has \(x \geqslant 1\), \(b_L \leqslant x\), and \(y \geqslant \max \{1, L^{d + 1} 2^{w_d} \}\); the last condition holds eventually because \(y \to \infty\) with \(x\). In particular \(0 < y \leqslant 2 x\). After multiplying by the positive denominators the tail comparison is equivalent to

\(\displaystyle L^{d + 1} y^{w_d} \leqslant yx^{w_d} .\)

The left side is at most \(L^{d + 1} 2^{w_d} x^{w_d}\), which is at most the right side because \(y \geqslant L^{d + 1} 2^{w_d}\). No sign condition on the shift \(b_L\) is used. The remaining conditions at a fixed stage-profile pair are just that \(x\) exceed the fixed profile cost and the finitely many credits requested at degrees \(d\) below that profile. They therefore hold eventually as well. For a fixed stage, only that stage and its adjacent predecessor are tested; the conjunction is finite. Choose a threshold for every stage and replace these thresholds by their prefix maxima, also taking the maximum with the original entrance delay. The new entrance delay is monotone, dominates the old one, and satisfies every adjacent-stage inequality whenever the actual delay exceeds it. This proves the delay calibration rather than assuming a single threshold works for all stages at once.

At the selected history caller, these are the three eventual conditions needed by the marked budget package. For each fixed degree \(d\), the section credit is queried at the profile of the actual stage \(J (N)\). The strict reference-depth margin implies \(d \leqslant J (N)\) for all sufficiently large \(N\). The purchased stage table supplies the credit and flight inequality at that stage, evaluated at its preceding block side \(L_{N - 1}\); this side is at least the stage threshold \(n_{J (N)}\) provided the thresholds \(n_i\) do not exceed the floor length of stage \(i\), which bounds from below every block side used at that stage. This comparison of thresholds with floor lengths is an explicit condition on the schedule of the caller; it is not proved in this section. The delay purchase supplies the allowance comparison. At level \(N\) the selected coupling \(\hat{\beta}_N\) is at least the entrance delay of the stage \(J (N + 1)\), and \(J (N) \leqslant J (N + 1) \leqslant J (N) + 1\), so the stage \(J (N + 1)\) and the profile \(J (N)\) form one of the adjacent pairs tested by the delay. Its credit clause at \(x = \hat{\beta}_N\) gives \(a_{d, J (N)} \leqslant \hat{\beta}_N\) for every \(d \leqslant J (N)\). The credit \(b_N = a_{d, J (N)}\) is thus nonnegative and no larger than the running allowance \(\hat{\beta}_N\). The tail clause of the same delay is used separately: with the schedule identity

\(\displaystyle \hat{\beta}_{i + 1} = \beta_F (\hat{\beta}_i, L_i) = \hat{\beta}_i + c_G \log L_i,\)

where \(c_G = s_G\) is the slope constant (12.86) of the gauge group, it reads

\(\displaystyle \hat{\beta}_{i + 1}^{- 1} L_i^{d + 1} \hspace{0.17em} \textsf{f}_d (\hat{\beta}_i) \leqslant \textsf{f}_d (\hat{\beta}_{i + 1}) \qquad (d \leqslant J (i)) .\) (13.5)

This is the height recurrence \(\phi_i L_i^{d + 1} \zeta_{d, i} \leqslant \zeta_{d, i + 1}\) consumed in Subsection 13.9, for the public choices \(\phi_i = \hat{\beta}_{i + 1}^{- 1}\) and \(\zeta_{d, i} = \textsf{f}_d (\hat{\beta}_i)\). Thus, at every sufficiently late stage for this fixed degree, there are simultaneously a valid derivative credit, the allowance bound, and the sphere/flight bound. These are exactly the three fields of the marked budget package used by the selected field-calibration call. It uses the same fixed marked section throughout: the delay changes available budget, not the section or its stencil.

13.6.3A small coordinate error gives an invertible calibration

The section is now fixed. At a given cutoff, the field calculation produces, for each coefficient basis vector \(e_j\), a proposed correction \(u_j\) satisfying

\(\displaystyle \|e_j - u_j \|_1 \leqslant \delta_d,\)

where \(\|x\|_1\) is the sum of the absolute values of the finitely many coordinates and \(\delta_d = (2\mathcal{O}_d^2)^{- 1}\). The required linear calibration is not inferred from columnwise closeness alone; the factor \(\mathcal{O}_d\) accounts for the finite symmetry and the change from the coordinate basis to the section's coefficient coordinates.

Lemma 13.13. (Robust finite-dimensional calibration) Let \(E\) be the coefficient space of the chosen frame, with its \(\ell^1\) norm. Let \(\mathcal{O} \geqslant 1\) bound both the sum of the chip coefficients and the \(\ell^1\) norm of each symmetry action on the frame. For columns \(u_j\), define the repainting map by

\(\displaystyle Dx = \sum_{(g, j)} \chi_{g, j} (x) \hspace{0.17em} \rho_g u_j, \qquad x = \sum_{(g, j)} \chi_{g, j} (x) \hspace{0.17em} \rho_g e_j,\)

where \(g\) ranges over the finite signed/conjugation symmetry list, \(\rho_g\) is its action on the coefficient frame, and the fixed complex- linear chip coefficients obey \(\sum_{g, j} | \chi_{g, j} (x) | \leqslant \mathcal{O} \|x\|_1\). If \(\|e_j - u_j \|_1 \leqslant (2\mathcal{O}^2)^{- 1}\) for every basis vector, then \(D\) is invertible and \(\|D^{- 1} \|_{1 \to 1} \leqslant 2\).

Proof. Subtracting the two repainting formulas gives \((I - D) x = \sum_{g, j} \chi_{g, j} (x) \rho_g (e_j - u_j)\). The sum of chip moduli is at most \(\mathcal{O} \|x\|_1\), and applying the symmetry to a column error costs at most another factor \(\mathcal{O}\). Consequently

\(\displaystyle \|(I - D) x\|_1 \leqslant \mathcal{O}^2 (2\mathcal{O}^2)^{- 1} \|x\|_1 = \tfrac{1}{2} \|x\|_1 .\)

The triangle inequality gives \(\|Dx\|_1 \geqslant \frac{1}{2} \|x\|_1\); therefore \(D\) is injective. Since \(E\) is finite dimensional, it is surjective and invertible. The same lower bound with \(x = D^{- 1} y\) gives \(\|D^{- 1} y\|_1 \leqslant 2 \|y\|_1\).\(\Box\)

13.6.4Application to the selected field calibration

We now state how the fixed section is compared, at each cutoff, with the local section of a selected family. The statement assembles two results proved later, the one-body invitation of Proposition 13.17 and the common-trajectory calibration of Proposition 13.19; neither of them uses the present subsection.

Fix a selected family \(C\) which makes the action, with its history, and a degree \(d\). Write \(F\) for the grade-\(d\) coefficient frame with basis \((e_j)_{j \in J_d}\), \(T\) for the local section of the family at degree \(d\), and \(V = V_d\) for the fixed section of Theorem 13.12, expressed in this frame and sound for the action of the family. At level \(N\) write \(a_N\) for the mesh, \(\theta_N\) for the budget threshold, \(c_N\) for the column count, \(\alpha_N\) for the support exponent, \(\sigma_N\) for the running scale, \(L_{N - 1}\) for the preceding block side, and \(\textsf{S}_N (q, b) = 2 + e^{81 \alpha_N} (b + c_N + 2 q)\) for the sphere bound. For a section \(W \in \{V, T\}\) and a coefficient vector \(v \in F\), the normalized origin field at \((N, p)\) is

\(\displaystyle \mathcal{O}^W_{N, p} (v) (U) = a_N^{- d} \hspace{0.17em} W \left( \theta_N^{- 1} \cdot_F v \right) (0, U),\)

where \(\cdot_F\) is the graded dilation multiplying the coordinate \(j\) by \(\theta_N^{- s_j}\). The inputs are the following.

  1. The eventual budget triple. There is a sequence \(b_N\) such that, for all sufficiently large \(N\): the section \(V\) has the credit of item (5) of Theorem 13.12 with constant \(b_N\) at the derivative order and power of the level-\(N\) budget; \(0 \leqslant b_N \leqslant \hat{\beta}_N\); and

    \(\displaystyle 2 (1 + c_N) \textsf{S}_N (q_d, b_N) \leqslant \textsf{P} (L_{N - 1}) \delta_d .\)

    For a schedule satisfying the floor and delay conditions stated in the preceding subsection, the triple follows with \(b_N = a_{d, J (N)}\); the discharge of those conditions on the selected public run is an open input.

  2. The paid selected-history record of Subsection 13.9, with its allowances \(u_N > 0\) and heights \(\zeta_{d, N} \geqslant 0\), and a terminal Keeper at every \((N, p)\) (Subsection 13.7). Assume also that the local forced-family attachment input of Proposition 13.17 holds for its correction at every sufficiently large \(N\) and every period \(p\).

  3. Slow growth of the reserves. Call a real sequence \(x_N\) slow if for every \(\varepsilon > 0\) one has \(|x_N | \leqslant e^{\varepsilon \sigma_N}\) for all sufficiently large \(N\) along the extraction order under consideration. The sequences \(u_N^{- 1}\) and \(\zeta_{d, N}\) are slow for every extraction order.

  4. The gauge group is simple, and the history has the opening schedule used for the centered root tests.

Put

\(\displaystyle \textsf{Z}_N = (\textsf{C} (V) + 2 \textsf{C} (T)) \text{frameWeight}_{d, N}, \qquad \text{frameWeight}_{d, N} = \sum_{j \in J_d} \theta_N^{- s_j},\)

where \(\textsf{C} (W) \geqslant 1\) denotes a fixed value-norm constant of the section \(W\): \(|W (v) (o, U) | \leqslant \textsf{C} (W) \|v\|_1\) for all coefficient vectors, sides, anchors and configurations. Such a constant exists because the finitely many basis columns of \(W\) are continuous functions on a compact configuration space. It is not a derivative bound, and \(\text{frameWeight}_{d, N}\) is not a number of sites: it bounds the \(\ell^1\) operator norm of the dilation \(\theta_N^{- 1} \cdot_F\). The bound attached to each constructed column is the explicit schedule amount

\(\displaystyle E_N = 4 (1 + c_N) \textsf{S}_N (q_d, b_N) + e^{81 (\alpha_N + 2)} \textsf{Z}_N \hspace{0.17em} \zeta_{d, N} \hspace{0.17em} u_N^{- 1} .\)

The first summand pays the local stencil and sphere terms; the second pays the forced correction after the reserve factor \(\zeta_{d, N} / u_N\).

Proposition 13.14. (Eventual invertible calibration of the fixed section) Under the four inputs above, for every sufficiently large \(N\) and every period \(p\) there are columns \(u_j = u_j (N, p) \in F\), \(j \in J_d\), such that:

  1. \(\|e_j - u_j \|_1 \leqslant \delta_d\) for every \(j\). Consequently the repainting map \(D_{N, p}\) built from these columns in Lemma 13.13 is invertible and \(\|D_{N, p}^{- 1} \|_{1 \to 1} \leqslant 2\).

  2. For every \(j\), every \(k\) with \(\text{ref} (d) \leqslant k < N\), every \(m\) opened at \(k\), and every motion \(s\) (a signed coordinate permutation, possibly composed with complex conjugation), the centered root test at depth \(m\) for the finite-volume law at \((N, p)\), in the notation of Subsection 13.9, satisfies

    \(\displaystyle \text{rootTest}_{m ; N, p} (s \cdot (\mathcal{O}^V_{N, p} (e_j) -\mathcal{O}^T_{N, p} (u_j))) \leqslant \text{rootFac} (d, m, k) \hspace{0.17em} E_N \hspace{0.17em} e^{- \gamma \sigma_N},\)

    with the constant \(\text{rootFac} (d, m, k)\) and the exponent \(\gamma = 1 / 8\) of Proposition 13.19. Its proof constructs the served representation for this residual, including all marker modes, in paragraph 13.9.3.

  3. The sequence \(E_N\) is slow for every extraction order.

The threshold in \(N\) depends on \(d\) but not on \(p\).

Proof. Fix \(N\) so large that the following hold; each holds for all sufficiently large \(N\), uniformly in \(p\). The three clauses of the budget triple hold at \(N\). One has \(N > \text{ref} (d)\). Every point \(z\) of the union \(X\) of the stencils of \(V\) and \(T\) satisfies \(20 (|z_i | + 1) \leqslant \text{side} (N, p)\) in each coordinate, because the sides tend to infinity uniformly in \(p\). Finally the three local reserve conditions of Lemma 13.16 and (13.12) hold for the stencil \(X\) and the two constants \(\textsf{C} (V), \textsf{C} (T)\); here simplicity of the gauge group is used, through \(\hat{\beta}_N \to \infty\) and the selected-budget radius formula in Subsection 13.8.

Fix \(p\) and \(j\). These are exactly the inputs of Proposition 13.17, with the terminal Keeper and local forced-family attachment, \(u_N > 0\) and \(\zeta_{d, N} \geqslant 0\) from the second input. It supplies a terminal coefficient \(Z_j\) with leading coordinate \(Z_j .x = e_j\), charge bound \(Q_N = \textsf{S}_N (q_d, b_N)\), budget \(E_N\), and field \(\mathcal{O}^V_{N, p} (e_j)\); these are the terminal insertion data. Since \(\text{ref} (d) < N\), Proposition 13.19 applies on the same family and history. It gives one label \(v_j\) with

\(\displaystyle \textsf{P} (L_{N - 1}) \hspace{0.17em} \|e_j - X_N^{- 1} v_j \|_1 \leqslant 2 (1 + c_N) Q_N\)

and the displayed root-test bounds for \(\mathcal{O}^V_{N, p} (e_j) - C.O (v_j)\), where \(X_N\) is the terminal calibration isomorphism of the family. Put \(u_j = X_N^{- 1} v_j\). By the definition of the family's observable, \(C.O (v_j) =\mathcal{O}^T_{N, p} (X_N^{- 1} v_j) =\mathcal{O}^T_{N, p} (u_j)\); this proves the second assertion. The flight inequality of the budget triple bounds the right side of the last display by \(\textsf{P} (L_{N - 1}) \delta_d\). Dividing by \(\textsf{P} (L_{N - 1}) > 0\) gives \(\|e_j - u_j \|_1 \leqslant \delta_d\), and Lemma 13.13 gives the invertibility and the inverse bound. The coefficient error is thus removed by one invertible linear regrading, shared by all labels at this cutoff.

For the slow-growth assertion, all the components of \(E_N\) are needed. The class of slow sequences is closed under finite sums and products, contains the constants, and contains every sequence dominated eventually by a slow sequence. The credit satisfies \(0 \leqslant b_N \leqslant \hat{\beta}_N\) eventually, and \(\hat{\beta}_N\) is slow because it is affine in \(\sigma_N\). The column count \(c_N\) is slow by the accumulated column-logarithm estimate. The opening schedule gives \(\alpha_N = o (\sigma_N)\), so \(e^{81 \alpha_N}\) and \(e^{81 (\alpha_N + 2)} = e^{162} e^{81 \alpha_N}\) are slow. Hence \(\textsf{S}_N (q_d, b_N)\) is slow. The inverse threshold \(\theta_N^{- 1}\) is slow, being bounded by \(\hat{\beta}_N\); therefore the finite sum \(\text{frameWeight}_{d, N}\) of its powers and \(\textsf{Z}_N\) are slow. Finally \(\zeta_{d, N}\) and \(u_N^{- 1}\) are slow by the third input. Each summand of \(E_N\) is a finite product of slow sequences. Omitting either of the two reserve bounds of the third input would not justify this conclusion.\(\Box\)

The section theorem and stage/delay arguments above prove the fixed-stencil family of fixed marked sections, all later credit requests, and, for a schedule satisfying the stated floor and delay conditions, the three eventual field-budget inputs. The local invitation and the common-trajectory calibration used in Proposition 13.14 remain separate substantive suppliers; they do not follow from existence of the section or from the inverse lemma. Their application requires the common-data matching and the remaining estimate boundary stated above.

13.7The marked observable at the terminal level

The terminal vacuum construction must also carry each local field and its forced profile. We give this construction before propagating the field down the selected history. All activities, supports and predicates in this section belong to that history.

13.7.1Reserved addresses and the missing part of the field

Fix a grade \(d\), a cutoff \(N\) and a period \(p\). Write \(R\) for the terminal representation, \(F\) for its grade-\(d\) coefficient frame, \(S\) for its sound local section, \(o = 0\) for its origin, \(Y\) for its footprint and \(0 < \theta \leqslant 1\) for its threshold. The forced activities are \(K_i\), with supports \(X_i\) and nonempty inventories \(I_i \subseteq X_i\). Their eligibility requires both the prescribed flags on \(X_i\) and the original additional predicate. A compatible collection has pairwise disjoint supports, and its inventories cover exactly the flagged sites.

The activity dictionary used in the startup and return constructions has, for every pair \(I \subseteq X\) with \(I \ne \varnothing\), an infinite sequence of reserved addresses. The address indexed by \((X, I, j)\) has support \(X\), inventory \(I\), and zero activity. Its additional predicate is precisely the condition that the flagged sites in \(X\) equal \(I\). Here is why these properties survive the construction. The dictionary embeds the old hard labels in one set of coordinates and reserves a disjoint set for every shape \((X, I)\). The new connected objects encode finite sets of these coordinates. A reserved singleton cannot be an image of an old object: that would put its reserved coordinate in the old coordinate embedding. Its coefficient therefore remains zero. Reading a singleton recovers exactly its support and inventory; its eligibility test is the original test for that one shape. This is the same operation in startup and every return. In particular it supplies the reserved addresses in the actual selected terminal representation.

We use these addresses to encode a finite compatible nonempty family \(b\) of old forced labels whose supports all meet \(Y\). Put

\(\displaystyle T_b = Y \cup \bigcup_{i \in b} X_i, \qquad I_b = \bigcup_{i \in b} I_i .\)

Choose the reserved address \(s (b)\) for shape \((T_b, I_b)\), with serial number encoding \((d, b)\). This is injective in \(b\). No old activity is overwritten: \(K_{s (b)} = 0\) before the marked coefficient is installed.

For \(v \in F\) let \(P_v\) be the primary, masked part of the section, and let

\(\displaystyle f_v (U) = S (\theta^{- 1} \cdot_F v) (o, U) - P_v (U) . \)

Here \(\cdot_F\) is the graded dilation: on the coordinate of a basis vector \(e_j\) of the frame it multiplies by \(\theta^{- s_j}\), where \(s_j\) is the factor grade of \(e_j\), the second entry of its grade \((r_j, s_j)\) in Subsection 13.6. The primary part retains the vacuum coordinate everywhere and the scaled nonvacuum part on healthy configurations. Hence \(f_v = 0\) when \(Y\) is healthy; otherwise it equals the scaled nonvacuum section. The map \(v \mapsto f_v\) is complex-linear, measurable, gauge invariant, and depends only on the field and flags in \(Y\). These assertions follow directly from the corresponding properties of the sound section and the mask. If \(q = \max_j s_j\) is the maximum factor grade and each axis section \(S (e_j) (o, \cdot)\) has modulus at most \(C_d \geqslant 1\), then

\(\displaystyle |f_v (U) | \leqslant C_d \theta^{- q} \| \text{core} \hspace{0.17em} v\|_1 . \) (13.6)

Indeed expand the nonvacuum coefficient in the finite axis basis and use \(0 < \theta \leqslant 1\), so \(\theta^{- s_j} \leqslant \theta^{- q}\) for every axis.

Define the new forced coefficient at \(s (b)\) by

\(\displaystyle J_v (s (b), U) =\boldsymbol{1}_{\{T_b \cap \text{flags} (U) = I_b \}} f_v (U) \prod_{i \in b} K_i (U), \) (13.7)

and set it to zero at all other addresses. Its support and inventory are exactly \(T_b, I_b\). When it is nonzero, the indicator ensures the original additional predicate of the reserved address. Old factors \(K_i\) retain all their own predicates. The dependence, invariance and measurability requirements thus hold on the original dictionary. The marked coefficient has lead \(v\), zero regular correction, and forced part \(J_v\).

13.7.2The exact density and forced profile

Consider an old compatible collection \(c\) at a configuration where \(f_v (U) \ne 0\). Split it uniquely into the labels meeting \(Y\) and the labels disjoint from \(Y\):

\(\displaystyle b = \{i \in c : X_i \cap Y \ne \varnothing\}, \qquad r = c \setminus b.\)

The set \(b\) is nonempty. Indeed \(Y\) is then not healthy, and a flagged site of \(Y\) lies in some inventory of \(c\), hence in its support. Replace \(b\) by \(s (b)\) and leave \(r\) unchanged. The result is compatible: its old remaining supports are disjoint from \(Y\) and from all supports in \(b\), hence from \(T_b\). Inventories still cover exactly the same flags. Conversely, from a compatible marked collection containing \(s (b)\), replace that address by \(b\). Its other supports are disjoint from \(T_b\), so this inverse restores precisely the original collection. These two operations are inverse before any summation.

For each paired term, (13.7) times its remaining old activity factors is \(f_v (U)\) times the original product. The following absolute estimate justifies summing these identities. Suppose \(k_i \geqslant 0\) majorizes \(|K_i |\) and has the actual weighted site bound

\(\displaystyle \sum_{i : x \in X_i} M (X_i) k_i \leqslant w, \qquad M (X) = \exp \{E \hspace{0.17em} \text{cover}_B (X)\}, \) (13.8)

where \(E \geqslant 0\) is the purchased support exponent. Because every \(X_i\) in \(b\) meets \(Y\), the connected covering inequality gives

\(\displaystyle M (T_b) \prod_{i \in b} k_i \leqslant M (Y) \prod_{i \in b} M (X_i) k_i .\)

Moreover the sum of \(M (X_i) k_i\) over labels meeting \(Y\) is at most \(|Y| w\): sum (13.8) over \(x \in Y\). For every finite set of such labels, the sum of products over its nonempty subsets is \(\prod_i (1 + M (X_i) k_i) - 1 \leqslant e^{|Y| w} - 1\). Increasing the finite sets and using nonnegativity proves

\(\displaystyle \sum_b M (T_b) \sup_U |J_v (s (b), U) | \leqslant C_d \theta^{- q} \| \text{core} \hspace{0.17em} v\|_1 M (Y) (e^{|Y|w} - 1) . \) (13.9)

Dropping the compatibility restrictions only enlarges this bound. It supplies a summable majorant for the new coefficients. The lawful old gas bounds supply the remaining dressing majorants, so the regrouping above is absolutely convergent. Its sum is exactly \(f_v\) times the old gas. Adding the primary contribution proves the pointwise identity

\(\displaystyle \text{density} (v) (U) = R (U) S (\theta^{- 1} \cdot_F v) (0, U) = a_N^d R (U) \hspace{0.17em} \text{initial}_{N, p} (v) (U) . \) (13.10)

Here \(R (U)\) denotes the value at \(U\) of the unmarked density of the terminal representation, and \(a_N\) is the mesh at level \(N\). The last equality is the definition of the physical normalization \(\text{initial}_{N, p} (v) = a_N^{- d} S (\theta^{- 1} \cdot_F v) (0, \cdot)\).

There is also an identity before multiplication by the unmarked gas. Group the forced coefficients according to their support and inventory. The address \(s (b)\) contributes exactly the expression in (13.7), and all addresses outside its image contribute zero. The bijection between \(b\) and the finite compatible families meeting \(Y\) identifies this profile with the attachment of the singleton response \(f_v\) on \(Y\). This is the forced-profile identity needed by the adjacent transfer construction. It follows from equality of the individual products and the summable majorant (13.9), not from the density identity (13.10).

The footprint, graded dilation and healthy mask commute with signed lattice motions. The sound section also commutes with the independent complex-conjugation action. Therefore \(f_v\) transforms into \(f_{g \cdot v}\), with the same scalar conjugation when appropriate. Reindexing the compatible collections preserves their supports and inventories and proves the same covariance for the attached profile. No equivariant choice of the serial numbers is needed: the profile sums over addresses. The density, coefficient index and forced profile thus have the common motion covariance required at terminal birth.

13.7.3Paying the birth cost before choosing the history

It remains to pay the factor in (13.9), uniformly in the period. The axis bound \(C_d\) comes from the fixed finite local section. Its stencil has cardinality at most a fixed \(R_d\), and, once the cover radius contains that stencil, \(\text{cover}_B (Y) \leqslant 1\). Thus the fee is at most

\(\displaystyle C_d \theta^{- q} e^E (e^{R_d w} - 1) . \) (13.11)

On a fixed profile stage, \(E, C_d, R_d, q\) are fixed. For the hatted coupling \(x\) the chosen threshold satisfies \(\theta^{- 1} \leqslant x\) once \(x \geqslant 1\), and the forced allowance is

\(\displaystyle w (x) = \exp \{- \exp ((\log \log x)^2 / 5)\}, \qquad 0 < w (x) \leqslant 1.\)

For each fixed integer \(n\), \(\sqrt{w (x)} x^n \to 0\). To see this, put \(u = \log \log x\). The logarithm of that product is \(- \tfrac{1}{2} e^{u^2 / 5} + ne^u\), which tends to \(- \infty\) because \(e^{u^2 / 5 - u} \to \infty\). Also \(e^z - 1 \leqslant ze^z\) for \(z \geqslant 0\), by integration of \(e^t\) over \([0, z]\). Consequently (13.11) is at most

\(\displaystyle K_d x^q w (x), \qquad K_d = C_d e^E R_d e^{R_d} .\)

Choose \(x \geqslant \max (1, K_d)\) and \(\sqrt{w (x)} x^{q + 1} \leqslant 1\). Then this last expression is at most \(\sqrt{w (x)}\). This proves the terminal remainder bound with charge \(\| \text{core} \hspace{0.17em} v\|_1\), uniformly in \(N, p\) at this profile stage.

At stage \(j\) there are only finitely many grades and earlier profiles through \(j\). Take a common maximum of their fee thresholds and stencil fit thresholds, then take running maxima in \(j\). Include this delay among the purchases of Subsection 13.4 before applying the common scale selection. At level \(m\), both its grade \(d \leqslant J (m)\) and its actual profile index \(J (m)\) lie in the finite list purchased at \(J (m + 1)\). The fee therefore fits at every admitted terminal level, on the same selected scale sequence. With (13.6)–(13.10), this supplies the terminal marked data and all of their bounds for the backward transfer construction.

13.7.4Summary of the terminal construction

For later reference we collect what has been proved, with its exact inputs. Fix \(d\), \(N\) and \(p\) as above.

Proposition 13.15. (Terminal marked coefficients) Assume the following.

  1. The terminal representation \(R\) of the selected history at \((N, p)\) is lawful: its forced labels satisfy the regularity, measurability and locality requirements of the dictionary, and their activities have majorants \(k_i \geqslant |K_i |\) obeying the weighted site bound (13.8) with some \(w > 0\).

  2. For every pair \(I \subseteq X\) with \(I \neq \varnothing\) the dictionary of \(R\) contains infinitely many reserved addresses with support \(X\), inventory \(I\) and zero activity, whose additional predicate holds at every configuration with \(X \cap \text{flags} (U) = I\).

  3. The local section \(S\) is sound, and \(|S (e_j) (0, U) | \leqslant C_d\) for all axes \(j\) and configurations \(U\), with \(C_d \geqslant 1\).

  4. The fee inequality \(C_d \theta^{- q} M (Y) (e^{|Y|w} - 1) \leqslant \sqrt{w}\) holds.

For \(v \in F\) let \(\mathcal{V} (v)\) be the marked coefficient with lead \(v\), zero regular correction and forced part \(J_v\) defined in (13.7). Then \(v \mapsto \mathcal{V} (v)\) is complex-linear in each of its three entries, and for every \(v\):

  1. each forced entry \(J_v (i, \cdot)\) is gauge invariant and measurable, depends only on the field and flags on the support of the address \(i\), and is nonzero only at configurations where that address is eligible for the original dictionary of \(R\);

  2. the forced entries have majorants with \(\sum_b M (T_b) \sup_U |J_v (s (b), U) | \leqslant \sqrt{w} \hspace{0.17em} \| \text{core} \hspace{0.17em} v\|_1\);

  3. the density identity (13.10) holds at every configuration;

  4. the forced profile of \(J_v\) on the activities of \(R\) equals the attachment of the singleton response \(f_v\) on the footprint \(Y\);

  5. for every signed lattice motion, with or without complex conjugation, the density of \(\mathcal{V} (v)\) transported by the motion is the density of \(\mathcal{V}\) at the transported label.

On the selected scale sequence, hypothesis (4) holds at every admitted terminal level \(N\), for every grade \(d \leqslant J (N)\) and every period, once the entrance delays include the fee thresholds chosen above.

Proof. Linearity holds because \(v \mapsto f_v\) is linear and (13.7) is linear in \(f_v\). Assertion (1) was verified after (13.7); the eligibility for the original dictionary uses the indicator in (13.7) and hypothesis (2). Assertion (2) is (13.9) combined with hypothesis (4). Assertions (3), (4) and (5) are respectively the identity (13.10), the forced-profile identity and the covariance proved in the second subsection. The final sentence is the conclusion of the third subsection.\(\Box\)

Hypothesis (2) is a property of the selected history, not of the construction in this section. The reserved-address property is an input here; the first subsection only recalls why the dictionary can carry such addresses, and the realization of the property on the selected history must be supplied by the history-selection construction. Hypothesis (1) is likewise a property of the selected history. The inclusion of the fee thresholds among the entrance delays, used in the final sentence of the proposition, is a condition on the schedule which is not discharged here. These hypotheses are recorded as inputs and are consumed, together with the five conclusions, by the backward constructions of Sections 13.10 and 13.9.

13.8Forced remainder bounds for a marked one-body insertion

This unit closes two finite estimates used when a marked label is inserted at the terminal scale of a selected history. The first is the smallness of the forced reserve relative to a fixed section and its finite frame. The second converts a paid local section into the exact coefficient and remainder data called a terminal insertion. It keeps the actual one-body insertion, its normalization, and its exponential support bound visible. The later recentering of this terminal insertion through all earlier scales is a separate step and is not claimed here.

13.8.1The forced reserve beats every fixed frame bound

Let \(F_d\) be a finite frame with grades \((r_j, s_j)\), \(j \in J_d\), and set

\(\displaystyle M_d = |J_d |, \qquad k_d = \max_{j \in J_d} s_j .\)

For a budget threshold \(\vartheta_N \in (0, 1]\), define the finite seat bound

\(\displaystyle \text{frameWeight}_{d, N} = \sum_{j \in J_d} \vartheta_N^{- s_j} .\)

Assume the scale parameter \(\beta_N \to \infty\) and \(\vartheta_N^{- 1} \leqslant \beta_N\). Define

\(\displaystyle \text{forcedReserve} (x) = \exp \hspace{-0.17em} \left( - \exp \hspace{-0.17em} \left( \frac{(\log \log x)^2}{5} \right) \right) \quad (x > 1), \qquad \mathfrak{f}_N = \text{forcedReserve} (\beta_N),\)

and, for fixed \(x, y \geqslant 0\),

\(\displaystyle \text{probeBound}_{d, N} (x, y) = (x + 2 y) \text{frameWeight}_{d, N} .\)

Lemma 13.16. (Reserve versus a fixed section) For every fixed \(d\) and \(x, y \geqslant 0\),

\(\displaystyle \sqrt{\mathfrak{f}_N} \hspace{0.17em} \text{probeBound}_{d, N} (x, y) \leqslant 1\)

for all sufficiently large \(N\). For every fixed finite stencil \(X\) on the cover lattice,

\(\displaystyle | \text{regional} (X, 0) | \hspace{0.17em} \mathfrak{f}_N \leqslant 1\)

for all sufficiently large \(N\) as well.

Proof. Write \(M = M_d\), \(k = k_d\), and \(A = x + 2 y\). Since each \(s_j \leqslant k\) and \(\vartheta_N^{- 1} \leqslant \beta_N\),

\(\displaystyle \text{frameWeight}_{d, N} \leqslant M \beta_N^k .\)

Choose \(N\) large enough that \(\beta_N \geqslant \max \{1, AM\}\). Then \(AM \beta_N^k \leqslant \beta_N^{k + 1}\), so it suffices that \(\sqrt{\text{forcedReserve} (\beta_N)} \hspace{0.17em} \beta_N^{k + 1} \leqslant 1\). For completeness, put \(v = \log \log \beta_N\), which tends to infinity with \(N\). The logarithm of the left side is

\(\displaystyle (k + 1) e^v - \tfrac{1}{2} e^{v^2 / 5} .\)

As \(v \to \infty\), \(v^2 / 5 - v \to \infty\), hence \(e^{v^2 / 5} \geqslant 2 (k + 1) e^v\) eventually. This proves the required super-polynomial decay and the first assertion.

Let \(m_X = |X|\). Regionalization is an image, so \(| \text{regional} (X, 0) | \leqslant m_X\). Eventually \(\beta_N \geqslant \max \{1, m_X \}\). The just-proved estimate with exponent one implies \(\sqrt{\mathfrak{f}_N} \hspace{0.17em} \beta_N \leqslant 1\), while \(0 <\mathfrak{f}_N \leqslant 1\); therefore

\(\displaystyle | \text{regional} (X, 0) | \mathfrak{f}_N \leqslant m_X \sqrt{\mathfrak{f}_N} \leqslant \beta_N \sqrt{\mathfrak{f}_N} \leqslant 1.\)

This proves the finite local allowance estimate.\(\Box\)

The volume-independent geometric cover estimate is also elementary. Let \(B_N\) be the radius in the budget's support norm and assume \(B_N \to \infty\). For fixed \(X\), every point of \(X\) is eventually in the radius-\(B_N\) cube. Consequently \(\text{regional} (X, 0)\) lies in the halo of the origin. The cube-tiling cover of that halo uses at most \(81\) support blocks, so

\(\displaystyle \text{coverNumber} (B_N, \text{regional} (X, 0)) \leqslant 81\) (13.12)

eventually. For the selected budget, \(B_N = \lceil (\log \beta_N)^2 \rceil\). Since \(\beta_N \to \infty\), also \(\log \beta_N \to \infty\), and the ceiling is at least its argument; hence \(B_N \to \infty\). For a different budget the same cover conclusion requires its own radius divergence. The three local conditions used below are therefore obtained with the same terminal threshold: the regional forced mass is at most one, its support cover number is at most \(81\), and the square-root reserve times the fixed storms bound is at most one.

13.8.2From a paid section to a terminal insertion

We record the exact one-body statement. Let \(C\) be a selected finite-volume family, \(N\) its terminal level, \(p\) the period, and \(d\) a label degree. Write \(F = F_d\), \(T\) for the fixed local section, \(V\) for the purchased section, \(\theta_N \in (0, 1]\) for the terminal budget threshold, \(w_N =\mathfrak{f}_N\) for the forced allowance, \(u_N > 0\) for the startup allowance, and \(\zeta_{d, N} \geqslant 0\) for the mark budget. Let \(R_N = C. \text{path.rep} (N, p, N)\) be the terminal representative, \(\alpha_N\) its support exponent, \(a_N > 0\) the physical lattice spacing, and \(B_N\) the support radius in the terminal budget point. Put

\(\displaystyle \begin{array}{rl} X & = \text{stencil} (V) \cup \text{stencil} (T),\\ D & = \text{regional} (X, 0), \qquad c_N = \text{column count},\\ s_N & = \text{probeBound}_{d, N} (\mathcal{B}(V), \mathcal{B}(T)) . \end{array}\)

where the two bounds are the global value-norm constants of \(V\) and \(T\); they are distinct from the derivative credit \(b_N\). Assume \(\alpha_N \geqslant 0\) and the following exact local inputs:

  1. \(C\) is a selected family which makes the action and \(C. \text{ref} (d) \leqslant N\). The section \(V\) is sound, and its Credit holds at the terminal order and power with a nonnegative bound \(b_N\).

  2. Every \(z \in X\) satisfies \(20 (|z_j | + 1) \leqslant \text{side} (N, p)\) for each coordinate \(j\). The local regional bounds are \(0 < w_N\), \(|D| w_N \leqslant 1\), and \(\text{coverNumber} (B_N, D) \leqslant 81\).

  3. The selected terminal representative is lawful and has a terminal Keeper: an injective assignment of an address of its dictionary to every triple \((X, I, n)\) with \(\varnothing \neq I \subseteq X\) and \(n \in \mathbb{N}\), with support \(X\) and inventory \(I\), whose additional predicate holds at every configuration with \(X \cap \text{flags} (U) = I\). This is the reserved-address part of hypothesis (2) of Proposition 13.15, with its zero-activity requirement omitted. Also \(u_N > 0\), \(\zeta_{d, N} \geqslant 0\), and \(\sqrt{w_N} s_N \leqslant 1\).

  4. At the terminal budget point, every placed basis column of \(T\) is regular on its footprint and has derivative bound \(c_N \theta_N^{s_j}\) for grade \(s_j\); this is the selected-family bound clause.

  5. The local forced-family attachment applies to the terminal representation and \(D\): for every basis vector \(e_j\), the gauge-invariant, measurable local correction \(b_0\) constructed below, vanishing when \(D\) is healthy and bounded by \(s_N\), it supplies a forced family with gas-mark sum \(b_0\) times the activity gas density, the prescribed hook profile, and bound at most \(\mathcal{B}_{\text{merge}} (D, s_N)\) defined below. This is a separate input for this proposition, realized for the selected terminal representation by the reserved-address construction in its proof.

For \(v \in F\), define the normalized cylinder of the purchased section by

\(\displaystyle \mathcal{O}_{N, p} (v) (U) = (a_N^d)^{- 1} V. \text{places} (F. \text{scaled} (\theta_N^{- 1}) v, 0, U) .\)

For a basis vector \(e_j\) of \(F\), define

\(\displaystyle \begin{array}{rl} Q_N & = 2 + e^{81 \alpha_N} (b_N + c_N + 2| X|),\\ E_N & = 4 (1 + c_N) Q_N + e^{81 (\alpha_N + 2)} s_N \frac{\zeta_{d, N}}{u_N} . \end{array}\)

Proposition 13.17. (One-body marked invitation) Under these inputs, put \(r_N = b_N + c_N\) (the regular bound), \(\kappa_N = \text{coverNumber} (B_N, D) \leqslant 81\), and

\(\displaystyle \begin{array}{rl} t_N & = e^{\alpha_N \kappa_N} r_N + 2 |D| \sqrt{w_N} \hspace{0.17em} e^{\alpha_N \kappa_N} s_N,\\ \bar{A}_N & = e^{81 (\alpha_N + 2)} s_N . \end{array}\)

Then for each basis vector \(e_j\) there is a coefficient record \(Z_j\) with the following fields:

\(\displaystyle \begin{array}{rl} Z_j .x & = e_j,\\ & Z_j . \text{Format} (0, C. \text{params}, R_N),\\ & Z_j . \text{remBound} (0, R_N, \sqrt{w_N}, t_N),\\ F. \text{coeffSize} (Z_j .x) + t_N & \leqslant Q_N, \end{array}\)

and \(0 < Q_N\). Since a remainder bound persists when its bound is increased, the same holds with \(t_N\) replaced by the larger envelope \(e^{81 \alpha_N} r_N + 2 |D| \sqrt{w_N} \hspace{0.17em} e^{81 \alpha_N} s_N\), which also satisfies the charge inequality. There are an attenuation \(S_j\) and an actual reserved mass

\(\displaystyle A_j = e^{(\alpha_N + 2) \text{coverNumber} (B_N, D)} s_N\)

with \(\text{Attainment} (R_N .p, 0, S_j, A_j)\) and \(A_j \zeta_{d, N} \leqslant u_N E_N\), and

\(\displaystyle \text{profile} (R_N . \text{activities}, Z_j . \text{forced}) = \text{hook} (R_N . \text{activities}, R_N .p. \text{threshold}, S_j) .\)

For every configuration \(U\), its density and continuity clauses are

\(\displaystyle \begin{array}{rl} Z_j . \text{density} (0, T, R_N, U) & = (a_N^d) R_N . \text{eval} (U) b_j (U),\\ b_j (U) & = (a_N^d)^{- 1} V. \text{places} (F. \text{scaled} (\theta_N^{- 1}) e_j, 0, U),\\ & \hspace{25mm} b_j \text{ is continuous} . \end{array}\)

The reserved-mass bound is

\(\displaystyle \bar{A}_N \zeta_{d, N} \leqslant u_N E_N, \qquad 4 (1 + c_N) Q_N \leqslant E_N .\)

Equivalently, these are the terminal insertion fields used by the selected field calibration.

Proof. Fix \(j\) and put

\(\displaystyle a = F. \text{scaled} (\theta_N^{- 1}) e_j, \qquad z = F. \text{coords}^{- 1} (a) .\)

Thus \(a\) is the dilated coefficient vector and \(z\) the label with these coordinates. By the inverse-coordinate identity,

\(\displaystyle F. \text{scaled} (\theta_N) \hspace{0.17em} F. \text{coords} (z) = e_j .\)

Set \(y = V. \text{places} (a, 0, \cdot)\). The pointwise section \(V. \text{places} (a, 0, \cdot)\) reads only the translated stencil of \(V\); by the stencil inclusion, this is contained in \(D\). The margin assumption implies that the placement is nonwrapping: each coordinate of every stencil point has absolute value strictly smaller than one twentieth of the torus side, so the translated stencil has a unique representative in the centered box. Thus the placement reads only the field on \(D\) without wrapping. The same statements apply to \(T\).

The Credit estimate for \(V\) applies to each basis column on its footprint. For the rescaled axis \(a = F. \text{scaled} (\theta_N^{- 1}) e_j\), only column \(j\) is nonzero; its coefficient has modulus \(\theta_N^{- s_j}\). The terminal Credit gives every ordered derivative test admitted by the terminal budget, on its healthy translated footprint, the bound \(b_N \theta_N^{s_j}\) for the unscaled column. Scaling cancels these factors, so \(y = V. \text{places} (a, 0, \cdot)\) has the same regularity and derivative bound \(b_N\). The selected-family bound clause gives the identical conclusion for \(g = T. \text{places} (a, 0, \cdot)\) with bound \(c_N\). Regularity and derivative sup bounds are closed under subtraction, so \(r = y - g\) is regular on \(D\) and every such derivative is bounded by \(r_N = b_N + c_N\). This is the bound used in the regular part of the remainder. The global value-norm constants in \(s_N\) are different: the definition of \(\mathcal{B} (V)\) and finite-coordinate expansion give, for every coefficient vector \(v\),

\(\displaystyle |V. \text{places} (v, 0, U) | \leqslant \mathcal{B} (V) F. \text{coeffSize} (v),\)

and likewise for \(T\). The scaled-coefficient estimate gives the explicit envelopes

\(\displaystyle \|V. \text{places} (a, 0, U)\| \leqslant \mathcal{B} (V) \text{frameWeight}_{d, N}, \qquad \|T. \text{places} (a, 0, U)\| \leqslant \mathcal{B} (T) \text{frameWeight}_{d, N} .\)

These are the uniform pointwise bounds used in the residual estimate. The local insertion construction uses this read relation and the terminal Keeper to split the target into its unit coordinate, the core of the local section, and a residual which vanishes at the identity. With \(g = T. \text{places} (a, 0, \cdot)\) and residual \(r = y - g\), define

\(\displaystyle \begin{array}{rl} b_0 & = r - \text{mask}_D (r) + \xi - \text{mask}_A (\xi),\\ \xi & = T. \text{places} (F. \text{scaled} (\theta_N^{- 1}) F. \text{core} (e_j), 0, \cdot) . \end{array}\)

where \(A\) is the local-section footprint and each mask is the indicator of the corresponding healthy set. The read identity, unit normalization, and the projection condition in section soundness give the null-value and gauge-vanishing conditions needed for the residual \(r\): both \(y\) and \(g\) read the same label \(z\), so \(r\) vanishes at the identity configuration and its action jets through order \(d\) vanish. On the healthy set for \(D\), \(\text{mask}_D (r) = r\) and, since \(A \subseteq D\), \(\text{mask}_A (\xi) = \xi\); hence \(b_0 = 0\) there. Off that set, \(|r - \text{mask}_D (r) | \leqslant |r| \leqslant |y| + |g|\) and \(| \xi - \text{mask}_A (\xi) | \leqslant | \xi |\). The undilated vectors \(e_j\) and \(\text{core} \hspace{0.17em} e_j\) have coefficient size at most one; their dilations, the coefficient vectors of \(g\) and \(\xi\), therefore have coefficient size at most \(\text{frameWeight}_{d, N}\). The triangle inequality and the two section bounds then give the pointwise bound

\(\displaystyle |b_0 (U) | \leqslant \mathcal{B} (V) \text{frameWeight}_{d, N} + 2\mathcal{B} (T) \text{frameWeight}_{d, N} = s_N .\)

This is the exact “storms” majorant; the two local-section terms are charged separately.

The target entering the coefficient density is

\(\displaystyle b_j (U) = (a_N^d)^{- 1} y (U), \qquad y (U) = V. \text{places} (F. \text{scaled} (\theta_N^{- 1}) e_j, 0, U) .\)

Thus \(b_j =\mathcal{O}_{N, p} (e_j)\).

For the conditional construction, start with the unpriced terminal record and its coefficient vector \(e_j\). Its regular part is the section remainder on \(D\); the forced correction is supported on \(D\). To bound the correction, let

\(\displaystyle \mathcal{B}_{\text{merge}} (D, h) = e^{\alpha_N \text{coverNumber} (B_N, D)} h (e^{|D|w_N} - 1) .\)

When \(|D| w_N \leqslant 1\), the elementary inequality \(e^t - 1 \leqslant 2 t\) for \(0 \leqslant t \leqslant 1\) yields

\(\displaystyle \mathcal{B}_{\text{merge}} (D, h) \leqslant \sqrt{w_N} \hspace{0.17em} 2 |D| \sqrt{w_N} e^{\alpha_N \text{coverNumber} (B_N, D)} h.\)

The local forced-family attachment input is realized on the selected terminal representation by the reserved-address construction of Subsection 13.7. Here is its application to this correction, rather than only to the section supplement treated there. The region \(D\) contains the origin, and \(b_0\) is gauge invariant, measurable, determined by the field and flags on \(D\), zero where \(D\) is healthy, and bounded by \(s_N\). For each compatible nonempty finite family \(b\) of old forced labels whose supports meet \(D\), put

\(\displaystyle T_b = D \cup \bigcup_{i \in b} X_i, \qquad I_b = \bigcup_{i \in b} I_i,\)

choose its distinct reserved zero address \(s (b)\), and install

\(\displaystyle J (s (b), U) =\boldsymbol{1}_{\{T_b \cap \text{flags} (U) = I_b \}} b_0 (U) \prod_{i \in b} K_i (U) .\)

The original additional predicate, locality and gauge invariance hold exactly as for (13.7) in that section. Whenever \(b_0 (U) \ne 0\), some site of \(D\) is flagged. Thus any old compatible collection contributing to the gas has a nonempty subcollection meeting \(D\). Replacing that subcollection by \(s (b)\), or reversing this replacement, is the same bijection used there. It identifies the forced profile with the hook of the singleton response on \(D\) before multiplying by the unmarked gas. With \(M (X) = e^{\alpha_N \text{coverNumber} (B_N, X)}\), the selected weighted site bound gives

\(\displaystyle \sum_b M (T_b) \sup_U |J (s (b), U) | \leqslant s_N M (D) (e^{|D|w_N} - 1) =\mathcal{B}_{\text{merge}} (D, s_N) .\)

Indeed \(M (T_b) \leqslant M (D) \prod_{i \in b} M (X_i)\) and \(\sum_{i : X_i \cap D \ne \varnothing} M (X_i) \sup |K_i | \leqslant |D| w_N\); summing products over nonempty subsets gives the displayed bound. The lawful old gas bounds then justify the density regrouping absolutely. No property special to a section supplement was used. This supplies the attachment, its profile and its bound for the present \(b_0\). The attainment recorded in the statement says that the singleton response \(S_j\), equal to \(b_0\) on the support \(D\) and zero on other supports, has these properties and is bounded by \(s_N\); its reserved mass is by definition \(e^{(\alpha_N + 2) \text{coverNumber} (B_N, D)}\) times that bound. Add this family to the regular coefficient produced by the support split. Pointwise, the primary part contributes the unit coordinate plus the masked core section \(\text{mask}_A (\xi)\), the regular part contributes the masked residual \(\text{mask}_D (r)\), and the gas-mark sum contributes \(b_0\). Since the dilation fixes the unit coordinate and \(T\) has unit normalization, \(g = (e_j)_0 + \xi\), where \((e_j)_0\) is the unit coordinate of \(e_j\). Hence their sum is

\(\displaystyle (e_j)_0 + \text{mask}_A (\xi) + \text{mask}_D (r) + b_0 = (e_j)_0 + \xi + r = g + r = y,\)

the target. The regular remainder's bound is its ordinary mass plus the merge bound. This construction also gives an attainment with reserved mass

\(\displaystyle A_j = e^{(\alpha_N + 2) \text{coverNumber} (B_N, D)} s_N \leqslant e^{81 (\alpha_N + 2)} s_N .\)

The regular part of the remainder has support mass \(e^{\alpha_N \kappa_N} r_N\), where \(\kappa_N = \text{coverNumber} (B_N, D)\). The merge-estimate, in the \(\sqrt{w_N}\)-weighted remainder budget, contributes \(2 |D| \sqrt{w_N} e^{\alpha_N \kappa_N} s_N\). Their sum is \(t_N\). Since \(r_N = b_N + c_N\), \(\kappa_N \leqslant 81\), \(\alpha_N \geqslant 0\), \(|D| \leqslant |X|\), and \(\sqrt{w_N} s_N \leqslant 1\),

\(\displaystyle t_N \leqslant e^{81 \alpha_N} (b_N + c_N + 2| X|), \qquad 1 + t_N \leqslant 2 + e^{81 \alpha_N} (b_N + c_N + 2| X|) = Q_N .\)

Here the extra \(1\) is the coefficient size of \(e_j\); this proves the terminal insertion charge clause. The actual reserved mass is

\(\displaystyle A_j = e^{(\alpha_N + 2) \text{coverNumber} (B_N, D)} s_N,\)

and the cover bound gives \(A_j \leqslant \bar{A}_N\). Multiplying the latter envelope by \(\zeta_{d, N} / u_N\) gives the reserved-mass clause from the second summand of \(E_N\); the first summand gives \(4 (1 + c_N) Q_N \leqslant E_N\). This proves all the stated record fields.\(\Box\)

The proposition supplies the one-body coefficient and profile under its terminal margins, Keeper, Credit, regional bounds and attachment input. The selected field calibration in Proposition 13.19 uses one recentered label for all earlier-scale root tests. It additionally requires the selected transfer sequence's ordered adjacent-scale transitions, the backward induction, and the paid height and profile estimates. These do not follow from the local reserve and invitation arguments above. From these data and the terminal attainment, its proof constructs the served residual representation in paragraph 13.9.3.

13.9Calibration of a terminal marked field on its selected history

The local invitation in Subsection 13.8 is a terminal-scale construction. The public field calibration also needs one label correction that works at all earlier scales of the already selected history. This section follows that exact route: the marked lifting supplies the family of fixed marked sections, the terminal insertion supplies the final coefficient, and a backward induction through the selected transfer sequence relocates that coefficient. The history, interfaces, family, and transfer sequence remain fixed throughout.

13.9.1From the marked lifting to the family of fixed marked sections

The marked lifting produced in Subsection 13.5 is an equivariant marked lifting with the powered slot-degree bound. Applying the fixed-stencil construction of Theorem 13.12 gives, at each label degree \(d\), one finite section \(V_d\), its stencil, its regrading \(R_d\) and its credits. These choices are made before selecting a finite-volume history. On the public branch the same data are carried alongside the selected family. They do not replace the family's own local sections: at degree \(d\) the family keeps its fixed local section \(T_d\), which defines its observables \(C.O (v)\), and the fixed marked section \(V_d\) is compared with it on the selected history. The comparison is Proposition 13.14; its two ingredients are the one-body invitation of Proposition 13.17, whose hypotheses refer to the selected family and its terminal Keeper, and the common-trajectory calibration proved in this section.

The precise same-history obligation is that the credits, budget triple and terminal insertion of the fixed section \(V_d\) are attached to the selected family and its history. The subsequent argument does not replace that history by one chosen for each period, degree, or terminal volume.

13.9.2Finite backward calibration

Fix one selected history \(H\), period \(p\), terminal volume \(N\), label frame \(F\), action \(A\), section \(B\), and a transfer sequence \(M\) on this history. At scale \(i\), write \(\iota_i : F \to F\) for the transfer sequence index, \(\Pi_i (v)\) for its transported coefficient, and \(g_i (v)\) for its charge. A state at scale \(i\) consists of a primary \(\varpi_i \in F\), a residual coefficient \(Z_i\), and a bound \(b_i\). Its translated state at displacement \(v\) is

\(\displaystyle \mathcal{Z}_i (v) = Z_i + \Pi_i (\varpi_i + v), \qquad c_i (v) = b_i + g_i (\varpi_i + v) .\)

Call the state keyed if \(Z_i .x = 0\) and \(Z_i\) has the prescribed format and remainder bound. Write \(J_i\) for the history's clock index at scale \(i\); the schedule has \(J_{i + 1} \leqslant J_i + 1\). Assume that the transition from scale \(i + 1\) to \(i\) preserves keyedness, gives the exact density push identity for every \(v\), and gives the direction relation comparing the leading coordinates for every \(v\). It does not assert equality of those coordinates. Writing \(L_i\) for the physical side at step \(i\), \(e_i = \text{stepSmall} (L_i)\), and \(K_i = W (J_{i + 1})\), for each displacement \(v\) there is an invertible map \(B_{i, v}\) such that

\(\displaystyle \begin{array}{c} \|B_{i, v} x\|_1, \|B_{i, v}^{- 1} x\|_1 \leqslant K_i \|x\|_1, \qquad c_i (v), t_{i + 1} (v) \geqslant 0,\\ \text{core} (B_{i, v} x) = B_{i, v} (\text{core} \hspace{0.17em} x),\\ \|x_i (v) - B_{i, v} (\textsf{D}^{\text{gr}}_{d, L_i} (x_{i + 1} (v)))\|_1 \leqslant e_i (\| \text{core} (x_{i + 1} (v))\|_1 + t_{i + 1} (v)),\\ c_i (v) \leqslant K_i \| \text{core} (\textsf{D}^{\text{gr}}_{d, L_i} (x_{i + 1} (v))) \|_1 + e_i t_{i + 1} (v) . \end{array}\)

Here \(W \geqslant 1\) is a fixed nondecreasing bound function on stages, \(\text{stepSmall} (L) = L^{- 3 / 8}\), \(\text{core} \hspace{0.17em} x\) is \(x\) with its unit coordinate deleted, and \(\textsf{D}^{\text{gr}}_{d, L}\) multiplies the coordinate of a basis vector of grade \((r_j, s_j)\) by \(L^{d - r_j} \geqslant 1\). Further, \(x_i (v), x_{i + 1} (v)\) are the leading coordinates of the translated states, and \(t_{i + 1} (v) = c_{i + 1} (v)\) is the upper state's nonnegative bound. More explicitly, these coordinates are the corresponding transfer sequence-index values \(\iota_i (\varpi_i + v)\) and \(\iota_{i + 1} (\varpi_{i + 1} + v)\); the displayed direction inequality, not an equality, compares them. The maps may depend on \(v\), but the displayed constants do not. The density identity is \(\rho_i (v) = L_i^d \hspace{0.17em} \textsf{P}_i \rho_{i + 1} (v)\), with \(\textsf{P}_i\) exactly the transition push in the history. The finite response decomposition supplies the weighted support estimate for the specified exponential entries and forced profiles. Every bound is required at every displacement, not only at the base displacement \(v = 0\).

For clarity, the paid one-step input has the following content. At scale \(i\) put \(C_i = \text{columns} (i)\). The tuned transfer record has a number \(\eta_i \geqslant 0\) with \((1 + 2 C_i) \eta_i \leqslant e_i\). For every keyed upper coefficient \(U\) with zero leading coordinate, format, and bound \(q\), it provides a finite insertion support and a coefficient correction whose leading error cost is at most \(\eta_i q\), whose remainder cost is at most \(\eta_i q\), and whose support charge is at most \(2 q\). The associated transfer witness is a finite insertion support that works for every displacement and compares the upper and lower forced profiles, exponential entries, and charges. The one-step construction takes the correction coefficient \(V\), sets \(h = \iota_i^{- 1} V.x\), and uses

\(\displaystyle \varpi_i = \varpi_{i + 1} + h, \qquad Z_i = V + \Pi_i (- h), \qquad b_i = \eta_i q + g_i (- h) .\)

The leading coordinate of \(Z_i\) vanishes because \(V.x + \iota_i (- h) = 0\). The format and remainder bounds follow by adding the transfer record coefficient to the earned transported coefficient. For every \(v\), the linear density readout cancels the two terms at \(h\) and \(- h\); the remaining density is exactly the transition push of the upper state's density. The direction estimate follows from the transfer record's paid direction bounds, the transfer sequence cone estimate \(g_i (\pm h) \leqslant C_i \eta_i q\), the transfer witness charge comparison, and \((1 + 2 C_i) \eta_i \leqslant e_i\). Finally, union the two finite insertion supports and add their response decomposition inequalities; this gives one common finite support for all \(v\). This is the precise one-step content used in the finite descent.

Lemma 13.18. (Backward propagation of one terminal coefficient) Let \(0 \leqslant r \leqslant l \leqslant m \leqslant N\). Suppose the history is selected and the same transfer sequence is admissible and responds at every level from \(r\) through \(N\). Suppose its one-step response on each interface \(D_i\), \(l \leqslant i < m\), is the response described above: for every keyed input it supplies a keyed output, the all-displacement density identity and direction bound, and a finite response decomposition witness, with the tuning and transfer record bounds stated there. At level \(m\), let \(Z\) be a coefficient with the required format, remainder bound \(t\), and

\(\displaystyle \|Z.x\|_1 + t \leqslant w.\)

Assume also the scale room and bank budgets. Then there are keyed states \((\varpi_i, Z_i, b_i)\) for \(l \leqslant i \leqslant m\) such that:

  1. the terminal state is the state obtained by adjoining \(Z\) to the level-\(m\) transfer sequence and subtracting the transfer sequence's primary which represents \(Z.x\);

  2. every adjacent pair is related by the supplied response on \(D_i\);

  3. with \(\varpi_l\) the bottom primary, evaluate the translated states at the displacement \(- \varpi_l\): set \(x_i =\mathcal{Z}_i (- \varpi_l) .x\) and \(c_i = c_i (- \varpi_l) = b_i + g_i (\varpi_i - \varpi_l)\). Then, for every \(l \leqslant i \leqslant m\),

    \(\displaystyle \exp \hspace{-0.17em} \left( \frac{\sigma_m - \sigma_i}{8} \right) (c_i +\|x_i \|_1) \leqslant 4 (1 + C_m) w, \qquad C_m = \text{columns} (m),\)

    where \(\sigma_i\) is the history scale parameter;

  4. with the same \(x_i, c_i\), and with \(C_i = \text{columns} (i)\) and \(s_i = L_i^{1 / 8}\),

    \(\displaystyle \begin{array}{c} 32 (1 + C_i)^2 \|x_i \|_1 \leqslant c_i \quad (l \leqslant i \leqslant m), \qquad s_i \|x_{i + 1} \|_1 \leqslant c_{i + 1} \quad (l \leqslant i < m),\\ c_m \leqslant 2 (1 + C_m) w. \end{array}\)

The terminal state has, for every \(v \in F\), exact identities

\(\displaystyle \mathcal{Z}_m (v) .x = Z.x + \iota_m (v), \qquad \mathcal{Z}_m (v) . \text{density} = Z. \text{density} + \Pi_m (v) . \text{density},\)

and its terminal bound obeys

\(\displaystyle c_m (v) \leqslant w + C_m (w +\|\mathcal{Z}_m (v) .x\|_1), \qquad C_m = \text{columns} (m) .\)

Proof. At the terminal level set \(a = \iota_m^{- 1} (Z.x)\). In the public application \(m = N\), and the identity-index condition at the terminal scale gives \((M. \text{tone} \hspace{0.17em} N) . \text{index} = \text{id}\): its transported coefficient has leading value \(v\) at every label \(v\). Thus in that application \(\iota_N (v) = v\). Form the residual coefficient by adding to \(Z\) the played field \(\Pi_m (- a)\), and set its bound to \(t + g_m (- a)\). Its leading coordinate is zero because \(\Pi_m (- a) .x = \iota_m (- a) = - Z.x\). This proves keyedness. For displacement \(v\), linearity of the transfer sequence readout gives

\(\displaystyle (Z + \Pi_m (- a) + \Pi_m (a + v)) .x = Z.x + \iota_m (v),\)

and linearity of its density gives cancellation of the \(- a\) and \(a\) terms, leaving \(Z\) plus the density of \(\Pi_m (v)\). These are exact identities.

For the charge bound, the transfer sequence's cone estimate gives \(g_m (x) \leqslant C_m \| \iota_m (x)\|_1\). Since \(\iota_m (a) = Z.x\) and \(\iota_m (a + v) =\mathcal{Z}_m (v) .x\), it follows that
