Choose a comparison depth \(k\) and cutoff \(N \geqslant k\). Above \(k\), retain the original history. At \(k\), use its actual representation in the chosen local presentation. Below \(k\), set the coupling identically \(\beta_{N, p, k}\) and apply the preceding construction down to the lower edge of the comparison interval. The local scale parameter lies in \([s / 2, s]\), with \(s\) the clock coupling at \(k\), and the endpoint bound \(| \beta_{N, p, k} - s| \leqslant s / 8\) places this constant coupling in the purchased band \(x / 2 \leqslant \beta_{N, p, k} \leqslant 3 x\) throughout the interval: \(7 s / 8 \geqslant x / 2\) and \(9 s / 8 \leqslant 3 x\) for \(s / 2 \leqslant x \leqslant s\).

Use Proposition 13.38 for the presentation at \(k\), with \(a_0 = a_{\text{coast}}\). Its fixed-profile restrictions are included in the local purchases; for a finite adjacent-profile menu take the smaller positive admissible \(a_0\). The endpoint has exactly the original density, the prescribed path and clip, additive cost at most \(q_0\), and forced allowance \(G (s)\). It is this re-expressed endpoint that starts the induction.

Inductively, the input at a step has cost \(q_n\), the output has cost at most \(q_{n + 1}\) by (13.43), and their unnormalized densities satisfy (13.41) at that very same coupling. Only the new entry is installed at each backward step. Thus all entries already constructed, including the endpoint and its marked labels, are retained. Composition proves the density identities for the entire auxiliary history. The comparison with one or two marked sites uses the same component alphabet and the same hole normalization. Its leading and remainder estimates are separate from this unmarked construction; they must be verified on these actual densities before the rank tests can be applied.

13.12.7Steps across a stage boundary

The cost estimate above fixes one block ratio \(L\). A comparison window may contain levels of two consecutive stages. We state what a step whose fine and coarse levels lie in different stages has to be supplied with, and check that the numerical part of the construction then passes across the boundary.

Consider the step from the fine level \(m + 1\) to the coarse level \(m\), at the constant coupling \(\beta = \beta_{N, p, k}\), with stage pair \((J (m + 1), J (m))\) and input cost \(q_n\). It is the restarted step of this section: it uses the fixed coupling \(\beta\) and the new multiplier letters \(h_{\alpha}\), and its fine and coarse clips, budget radius and support radius \(B \leqslant M_s^2\) are those of the two levels at that pair. We use the following three hypotheses for that step.

    (V1)

    Component bounds. The unmarked coarse letters of the step other than the multiplier letters, built at the coupling \(\beta\) with the clips, radii and support exponent \(a\) of the pair, have weighted allowance at most \(\delta_n - \omega_n\) at the support weight. The component estimate of Subsection 8.2 applies to the alphabet enlarged by the multiplier letters.

    (V2)

    Path correction. With \(A_c\) the retained coarse path action of the coarse clip of the pair and \(\beta B_c\) the reference action of the fluctuation formula at the coupling \(\beta\), the masked local pieces of \(\beta B_c - A_c\) have anchored cost at most \(s^{- 1 / 200}\).

    (V3)

    Reassembly. The reassembly identity of Subsection 8.29 and the forced-exclusion identity hold for that enlarged alphabet, with exact flag coverage and the addresswise coarse forced activities of Proposition 14.2.

At a fixed ratio \(L\) these are the bounds used in the preceding subsections. Given (V1)–(V3), the output of the step is described by data of the same kind at the coarse level: a path action, a forced gas with its tests, an additive family of anchored cost at most \(q_{n + 1}\), and the push identity (13.41). That output does not refer to the stage of the fine level.

Lemma 13.39. (Stage changes inside the interval) Let the interval of the construction contain levels of the stages \(r\) and \(r - 1\) only. Suppose that (V1)–(V3) hold at every step of the interval, each for its own stage pair, which is one of \((r, r)\), \((r, r - 1)\) and \((r - 1, r - 1)\). Suppose also that the thresholds after which the eventual inequalities of the cost estimate hold are taken as the larger of those for \(L_r\) and \(L_{r - 1}\). Then the inductive joining argument above holds on the whole interval, with the same costs \(q_n\) and the same density identities.

Proof. The induction hypothesis at a step is an input of cost \(q_n\) with its density. The multiplier letters cost at most \(\omega_n\) by the footprint bound, in which the block ratio enters only through \(C_{L, a, t}\); take the larger of the two constants. By (V1) the unmarked allowance is at most \(\delta_n\), so the regular logarithm has cost at most \(16 \delta_n\). By (V2) and (13.40) the combined family has cost at most \(s^{- 1 / 200} + 16 \delta_n\), which is at most \(q_{n + 1}\) by (13.43). By (V3) the push identity (13.41) holds at that step. In \(h_1, h_2\) take \(L\) to be the smaller of \(L_{r - 1}, L_r\). Then (13.42) and (13.43) hold for every step beyond the later of the two thresholds, and the rest of the argument is unchanged.\(\Box\)

13.12.8Purchasing the actual adjacent steps

We now verify (V1)–(V3) for the local construction, rather than assuming them for a restarted history. The fixed input is the funded chart/stamp contract: the original path bounds and binding, the actual adjacent tubes and their metric floors, the room and chart parameters, and the local reserve choices of Subsection 14.11. The support purchases are made at the larger raw exponent and its reserve in Proposition 14.38. These are purchases on each stage pair, before the coupling and before the period; they do not assume any estimates on the restarted representations. As in the all-stage selection, a statement for a fixed pair below is made into a request at that pair, and the finite set of requests at a stage is intersected before choosing its ready time. No supremum of constants over infinitely many profiles is taken.

First record the path correction explicitly. On a coarse level with threshold \(\tau_x = x^{- 1 / 2} \text{hot} (x)\), support exponent \(a\) and radius \(B_c\), let \(C_c\) bound each of its cubic and quartic coefficient sums at path rate \(\sigma_c > 0\), and let \(\Gamma_c\) bound its quadratic loop sum. If \(a / B_c \leqslant \sigma_c\), the anchored bound of the correction \(\beta B_c^{\text{ref}} - A_c\) is at most

\(\displaystyle \beta e^{7 a} C_c \tau_x^3 + \beta e^{9 a} C_c \tau_x^4 + e^{5 a} \Gamma_c \tau_x^2 .\) (13.47)

Here \(B_c^{\text{ref}}\) denotes the reference base action, not the budget radius. Indeed its difference from the full path action consists of minus the cubic and quartic terms with coefficient \(\beta\), and minus the loop terms with coefficient one, with their original healthy masks. An order-\(j\) atom of true length \(d\) has cover at most \(1 + 2 j + d / B_c\). Its weighted value is therefore bounded by \(e^{a (1 + 2 j)} \tau_x^j\) times its coefficient weighted by \(e^{\sigma_c d}\). Sum these coefficient bounds at each anchor, and average the signed channels with their probability weights. This proves (13.47), including all winding paths. Since \(\Gamma_c \leqslant 2 \exp ((\log \log x)^2)\) and \(\beta \leqslant 4 x\), its right side is \(x^{- 1 / 2 + o (1)}\). Also \(a / B_c \leqslant \sigma_c\) eventually. Uniformly for \(x \in [s / 2, s]\), this proves (V2), with bound \(s^{- 1 / 200}\).

Proposition 13.40. (Closure of the restarted local construction) With the funded chart/stamp data just specified and the original endpoint bounds of Proposition 13.38, the restarted representations can be constructed throughout the comparison interval. At level \(i\), their path and clip are those of the original level, their coupling is \(\beta_{N, p, k}\), their additive value cost is at most \(q_{k - i}\), and their forced allowance is \(G (x_i) = \exp (- a_{\text{coast}} \text{hot} (x_i)^2)\), where \(x_i = \hat{\beta}_i\). Their neutral versions have the inherited law required by the actual local compiler. On every adjacent step the construction satisfies (V1)–(V3) on its own corrected alphabet and has the exact density identity (13.41). The upper and lower compiler estimates use this same alphabet and normalization. All thresholds are uniform in cutoff, period, fields and marked families with the fixed arity cap and bound parameters, with the fixed-profile dependence allowed by the funded contract.

Proof. Matching the geometric data. The clock estimate in the proof of Lemma 17.14 gives more than its stated half-band. Its upper bound for the total logarithmic scale loss is \(o (s_k)\), so, increasing the ready threshold, one has \(3 s / 4 \leqslant x_i \leqslant s\) on the whole interval. Since \(| \beta_{N, p, k} - s| \leqslant s / 8\), this gives

\(\displaystyle x_i / 2 \leqslant \beta_{N, p, k} \leqslant 2 x_i .\)

The clock relation on a step is exactly \(x_{i + 1} = \beta_F (x_i)\) for its own fine block size. Retiming gives the coarse threshold \(\text{fieldCut} (r (x_i))\), while the fine threshold is \(x_{i + 1}^{- 1 / 2} \text{hot} (x_{i + 1})\). The logarithmic radii have the required comparison \(B_f \leqslant 2 B_c\) eventually, and the budget wall dominates the fixed multiples of the local radius. These are the local radius and band conditions of the corrected compiler. Its remaining room and chart inequalities are the requests at the actual stage pair in the funded contract.

Retain the two original beds and the original tube at this step. Their path attachment, diagonal identity, tower roll, metric floors and path bounds are properties of those paths and clips, not of the additive or forced coefficients. Consequently the alongside certificate is unchanged when those coefficients are replaced. The induction below always retains the prescribed path and clip, so the upward path and high clip of this actual tube are exactly those of its incoming representation. This verifies the same-data requirement of the local handoff, including at a stage boundary; equality of the two stage indices is not used.

The incoming law. Start with Proposition 13.38. Inductively suppose the stated bounds hold at level \(i + 1\). Delete only its additive family. The forced activities, their eligibility tests, path and clip remain unchanged, and the additive cost is zero. This gives the neutral law with any nonnegative additive allowance, in particular the calendar allowance used by the inherited estimate. Its loop coefficient bound is the original \(\Gamma_{i + 1} \leqslant 2 H_{x_{i + 1}}\), which is eventually smaller than the required positive power of the local fine parameter. The genuine representation separately has value cost \(q_n\), where \(n = k - (i + 1)\). There is no assertion that \(q_n\) meets the much smaller calendar derivative allowance. The correction letters use only its value bound and their full local supports.

Bounds on this alphabet. Apply the actual local handoff to this tuple. Its matrix and both word inventories, owner bound, and all-matched warning estimate are uniform in the incoming field and store. In particular Lemma 14.12 bounds a product of warning factors and genuine remainder factors in the same Gaussian integral. Let \(m\) be the larger of \(G (x_i)^{- 2}\) and \(G (x_{i + 1})^{- 1}\), and let \(l\) be the larger unforced multiplier needed by the two compiler uses. The latter has \(\log l = O ((\log \log s)^2)\) by the upper calculation (\(l \leqslant \max (1, H_s^{q - 3})\)); the lower use has multiplier one. Fix these before introducing any marked family.

We verify the inflated body-product premise, including its site bound. Each forced body component contains an owner. Charge its factor \(m\) to such an owner, bounding it by \(m\) for each owner if there are several. For unforced components charge \(l\) to a constituent; bounding by \(l\) for every constituent only increases the bound. An owner of \(r\) seed shares is bounded by \(\exp [- K \text{hot} (x_{i + 1})^2 r]\) after its normalization is paid, with the positive margin \(K\) of the local handoff. Uniformly for \(x_i, x_{i + 1} \in [3 s / 4, s]\),

\(\displaystyle \frac{\text{hot} (x_i)^2}{\text{hot} (x_{i + 1})^2} \longrightarrow 1.\)

To check this, the difference of their \(\log \log\) arguments is \(O (1 / \log s)\); the mean value theorem applied to \(2 t^{3 / 2}\) bounds the difference of the logarithms by \(O (\sqrt{\log \log s} / \log s)\). Hence \(\log m \leqslant 4 a_{\text{coast}} \text{hot} (x_{i + 1})^2\) eventually. The purchased inequality \(2 a_{\text{coast}} \leqslant K / 4\) leaves at least half the owner suppression. The factor \(l\), the finite decoration costs and the packet count \(H_B D_B^{2 r}\) from Subsection 8.2 are absorbed in the remaining margin: their logarithms per seed are \(O ((\log \log s)^2 + \log B)\), whereas \(\text{hot} (x_{i + 1})^2\) dominates every fixed multiple of these quantities. Thus the inflated owner site bound is smaller than any fixed negative power of \(s\). The joint warning bound has the same property, even after the fixed halo counts and \(l\) are paid, by (8.18). Its exponent grows like a positive constant times \(\exp (\tfrac{1}{2} (\log \log s)^2)\), which dominates \(\log s\).

The remaining neutral auxiliary letters have site bound at most \(r (x_i)^{- 1 / 48}\) by the corrected exponent decomposition. Multiplication by \(l\) and fixed cover/counting costs changes this to \(s^{- 1 / 48 + o (1)}\). The projected old forced background has bound at most \(\mathcal{W} (B_c) L^4 T_E G (x_{i + 1})\), by (14.21) and its site version. This too is smaller than any fixed negative power of \(s\). The background need not itself pay \(m\): exact inventory coverage places every forced background inventory in a body owner component, which supplies that inflation, as in the proof of (14.25).

Finally, the genuine additive letters have uninflated footprint bound at most \(C (42 B_c + 1)^4 q_n\). All fixed powers of \(B_c\) and all factors \(l\) are \(\exp (O ((\log \log s)^2))\). Since \(A_s = \exp ((\log \log s)^3)\), each fixed product of these factors is at most \(A_s^{1 / 4}\) eventually. In particular the inflated additive site bound is at most \(q_n A_s^{1 / 4}\). There are finitely many such products in this argument; choose their thresholds together, uniformly for \(n \leqslant h\). The incoming bound (13.42) makes the combined site bound times every fixed radius polynomial tend to zero. The rooted-tree estimate of Subsection 14.4 therefore applies. For components containing an additive letter, root at such a letter; its tree estimate bounds their site sum by its inflated site bound, with the fixed support-conversion factors just paid. Enlarging to \(q_n A_s = \omega_n\) leaves room for these factors. Components with no additive letter have site bound \(s^{- 1 / 48 + o (1)}\), eventually at most \(\delta_n - \omega_n\): indeed \(q_n \geqslant s^{- 1 / 200}\) and \(\delta_n - \omega_n = q_n (A_s^3 - A_s)\). This proves (V1), with the specified separate allowances for the two classes. It also proves the inflated product estimate itself: use the pointwise owner and auxiliary bounds and the single joint warning event, and then group connected constituents. All these estimates use the full supports.

Reconstruction and renewal. Choose the scalar using the positive original loading, the additive family from the regular logarithm and the correction (13.47), and the forced family by Proposition 14.2. Its proof, applied to the genuine corrected body identity, supplies (V3). The absolute component bounds just proved justify its reassembly and every hole-deletion identity. Their normalization is the same one used by the mixed compiler. The path bound proves (V2), so the new additive cost is at most \(s^{- 1 / 200} + 16 \delta_n \leqslant q_{n + 1}\).

For completeness the hole corrections do not exhaust the forced margin. Their raised site cost is at most \(16 l \delta_n e^{16 \delta_n}\) by \(|e^{- c} - 1| \leqslant e^{|c|} |c|\). The path correction has been absorbed entirely into the coarse additive family, so there is no residual boundary factor. The component and raised-hole site costs are consequently \(s^{- 1 / 400 + o (1)}\) by (13.42). They satisfy the rooted smallness and normalization conditions of the mixed compiler, with its available \(H_s^{- 1 / 8}\) reserve. Its forced output is bounded by \(1 / m\), hence by \(G (x_i)^2 \leqslant G (x_i)\). Attach and sum at full addresses as in that proposition; this preserves the bound and all eligibility tests. The resulting neutral law has the retained original path bounds, zero additive cost and this new forced allowance. The symmetrization above supplies the motion properties of the genuine additive family. This proves the induction hypothesis at level \(i\) together with the exact push of the genuine incoming density.

Only the newly constructed entry at \(i\) is installed. Thus subsequent backward steps retain the endpoint and every already constructed higher entry. The equality of consecutive couplings makes them all \(\beta_{N, p, k}\), and composition gives the density identities on the same auxiliary course. For the three possible adjacent stage pairs, use their own positive path rates, floors and clips throughout and intersect their finitely many thresholds. The numerical argument of Lemma 13.39 now applies with (V1)–(V3) proved. Finally the alphabet, loading and all-hole identities were selected before any marked family. Decreasing the raw exponent and inflations gives the upper and lower uses on this same output, as asserted in Proposition 14.38.\(\Box\)

13.12.9One auxiliary family on the whole chart window

The family used in a chart is the one just constructed, including its re-expressed entry at the top. We record its budget and density at every level; equality of two endpoint representations is not needed. Put

\(\displaystyle b (x) = \lceil (\log x)^2 \rceil, \qquad x_j = \hat{\beta}_j, \qquad b_j = b (x_j), \qquad B_j = b_j^2, \qquad \tau_j = x_j^{- 1 / 2} \exp ((\log \log x_j)^{3 / 2}) .\)

The radius \(B_j\) is the representation's budget radius. It is distinct from the recording radius \(b (r (x_j))\) in the local Gaussian calculation. The retiming of Subsection 14.9 is chosen so that its recording threshold equals \(\tau_j\); it satisfies \(x_j \leqslant r (x_j) \leqslant x_j^2\) and \(b (r (x_j)) \leqslant 4 b_j\). Thus, for all large \(x_j\),

\(\displaystyle 25 b (r (x_j)) + 30 \leqslant b_j^2 \leqslant b (r (x_j))^2 .\)

The first inequality follows from \(100 b_j + 30 \leqslant b_j^2\) eventually, and the second from \(b_j \leqslant b (r (x_j))\). Also \(B_{j + 1} \leqslant 2 B_j\) eventually: on each fixed stage pair \(x_{j + 1} = x_j + c_G \log L_j\), so \(b_{j + 1} / b_j \to 1\). The finite adjacent-pair purchases make these inequalities simultaneous on the window. These are precisely the budget-radius conditions of Subsection 14.3. We can therefore use the exact budgets \((B_j, \tau_j)\), rather than merely comparable radii.

Proposition 13.41. (The retained auxiliary chart family) Fix a finite grade menu and an arc bound \(\bar{b} > 0\). Under the funded hypotheses of Proposition 13.40, for every sufficiently large level \(k\), every chart with arc \(0 < b \leqslant \bar{b}\), every \(N \geqslant k\) and every period \(p\), choose the endpoint presentation and then the backward steps once. Denote the resulting representations on \([w, k]\) by \(P_j\). They have budgets \((B_j, \tau_j)\), the original paths and stage clips, and common coupling \(\beta = \beta_{N, p, k}\). Their additive value costs are bounded by \(q_{k - j}\), and their densities are nonnegative.

Let \(D_k\) be the density of the original selected representation at \(k\), and define, using the selected kernels at the frozen coupling \(\beta\),

\(\displaystyle D_j =\mathcal{K}_{j, \beta} D_{j + 1} \quad (w \leqslant j < k) .\)

Then

\(\displaystyle \text{eval} (P_j) = D_j \quad (w \leqslant j \leqslant k), \qquad \int D_j \hspace{0.17em} d \mu_j = Z_{N, p} .\) (13.48)

All the local compiler and marked comparisons on this chart use these same \(P_j\) and the alphabets chosen in their construction.

Proof. At \(k\), Proposition 13.38 supplies exactly the budget \((b_k^2, \tau_k)\) while retaining the original path, clip, coupling and density. It supplies cost at most \(q_0\). In particular, \(P_k\) is a new presentation of \(D_k\), not an assertion that its smaller threshold equals the threshold of the original presentation.

Suppose the entries \(P_{i + 1}, \ldots, P_k\) have been fixed. Apply Proposition 13.40 to this very incoming \(P_{i + 1}\) and the original adjacent tube, with the coarse budget \((B_i, \tau_i)\) verified above. Retain its output as \(P_i\) and retain its corrected alphabet. That proposition gives the original coarse path and clip, coupling \(\beta\), the cost \(q_{k - i}\) and

\(\displaystyle \text{eval} (P_i) =\mathcal{K}_{i, \beta} \text{eval} (P_{i + 1}) .\)

No higher entry is changed. Finite descending induction proves all these assertions together, and the recursion defining \(D_i\) proves (13.48)'s first assertion. Positivity follows from the nonnegative endpoint and positive kernels, not from any sign claim about the individual terms of the expansion.

Each selected kernel has mass one in its coarse variable. The endpoint density is integrable on the finite-volume space; the bounded positive kernels preserve integrability. Fubini therefore gives \(\int D_i \hspace{0.17em} d \mu_i = \int D_{i + 1} \hspace{0.17em} d \mu_{i + 1}\). At \(k\) the original selected transition identities identify this integral with \(Z_{N, p}\). This proves the second assertion. Finally the local construction chooses its alphabet before the marked insertions and supplies both compiler uses on that alphabet. Keeping those choices at each step gives the last assertion. This is a construction of one family with its estimates; it makes no uniqueness claim for representations having the same density.\(\Box\)
